- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 8 · Phase 4): **Abandoning onboarding no longer leaves an identity document in storage.** Reachable before this change: `/onboarding` has no role gate (by design, since a buyer becomes a vendor through it), and `Onboarding.tsx` called `uploadKycDocument(user!.id, file)` the moment a file was picked. For a buyer that id is their own user id, so a PAN scan landed in `business-docs/<buyer-id>/kyc/…` with no `vendor_profiles` row and no page that could show or delete it. The same happened whenever a vendor removed a pick or replaced it.
  - **Chosen: upload at submit (option a), not a scheduled cleanup (b).** Onboarding keeps its state in memory only (no saved draft to resume), so nothing needs a stored path before submit, and a `File` object is enough. A cleanup job would need a service-role edge function plus cron, because direct deletes on `storage.objects` are refused, and it would delete identity documents on a timer. The old "blob: URL never persists" bug does not come back: nothing is stored until submit, and what is stored is a real `business-docs` path from `uploadKycDocument()`.
  - **Now:** picking PAN, GST or CIN attaches the file, checked against the "jpeg, png or pdf, up to 5MB" the copy states, and the row reads "Attached · uploaded when you submit". At submit each attached scan uploads, GST and CIN only when their number is given, since without a number no row is written. If the submit fails, `discardUnreferencedKycUploads()` removes whatever it uploaded that no `vendor_documents` row points at. It deliberately leaves referenced ones alone, because `saveVendorOnboarding()` can fail after writing the rows.
  - **Verified:** `vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts` 3/3 (demo-buyer, real `/onboarding`). The new assertion runs with PAN, GST and CIN attached and before submit: `business-docs/<id>/kyc` is empty. After submit, the existing assertions find all three `file_url`s as real private paths whose signed URLs resolve. `npm run typecheck` → 0 errors. Existing orphans: none. On 2026-09-11 every `business-docs/*/kyc` object belonged to a vendor profile and was referenced by a row.
- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 8 · Phase 3): **A vendor whose KYC document is rejected can replace it on `/kyc`, and it goes back into review.** Before, `/kyc` said "contact support", and onboarding, the only upload path, is closed once `onboarding_complete` is set.
  - **Shape, matched to the schema rather than invented.** A resubmission is a NEW `vendor_documents` row, never an edit of the rejected one: `vendor_documents_guard_review_columns()` refuses a vendor's change to `verified` / `rejection_reason` / `reviewed_*`, and forces a fresh INSERT to unreviewed, so the new row reads "awaiting review" by construction.
  - **One hygiene rule, not two.** Onboarding's delete-then-replace block is now a shared `replaceVendorDocuments()` in `src/lib/queries/vendorOnboarding.ts`, used by `saveVendorOnboarding()` and by the new `resubmitKycDocument()`. The new function uploads through `uploadKycDocument()`, so the same bucket and `${vendorId}/kyc/…` path rules apply, and removes the new object again if the row write fails. The shared helper now **inserts first** and deletes the superseded rows by id afterwards. Onboarding used to delete first, so a failed insert left a vendor with no row at all.
  - **Audit vs hygiene:** the superseded scan is removed, as onboarding already did, because an identity document should not sit in storage referenced by nothing. The rejection itself stays on record in the vendor's `kyc_rejected` notification, reason included.
  - **UI:** on a rejected row, "Upload a replacement PAN/GST/CIN" (JPEG, PNG or PDF up to 5 MB, the limits the onboarding copy already states). The page note now says a rejected document can be replaced there; adding a type never submitted still goes through support, because that needs the number too.
  - **Verified** (`tests/mp8-kyc-reupload.spec.ts`, 1/1, against both dev servers). The admin rejects the throwaway vendor's PAN, and the vendor replaces it through the new control. Afterwards the database has exactly one PAN row, a new id (`6f459a14…`), with `verified = false`, `reviewed_at = null` and a new path. Storage still has the replacement; the rejected `…/kyc/1788897227689-js2zvz.png` is gone. Cosora-Admin shows that PAN as "awaiting review" with no rejection, and its real Approve button then verifies it. Notifications: `kyc_rejected` (with the reason), then `kyc_approved`. Regression: `vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts` 3/3 with the shared helper in place. One earlier run lost its session at step 6, before anything this phase touches; an A/B with these changes stashed passed 3/3, and so did the rerun with them restored.
  - Also fixed: `vendor-my-store.spec.ts` still expected an "Aadhaar" row on `/kyc`, removed when Aadhaar was deferred. `npm run typecheck` → 0 errors.
- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 8 · Phase 2): **Vendor review counts and ratings are the real ones, and only a review can move them.** Product-level card numbers are unchanged, on Mitra's decision ("keep for now"): `products.rating_avg` / `reviews_count` / `sold_count` still feed New Arrivals, Search, For You and Trends cards.
  - **Recomputed.** `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_vendor_review_aggregates_truthful.sql` resets `vendor_profiles.rating_avg` / `reviews_count` with `sync_vendor_rating()`'s own formula. Four seeded vendors had never received a review, so that trigger had never run for them: Lucknow Chikankari Co. 4,800 / 4.7 → 0 / 0, Delhi Fashion Hub 318 / 4.6 → 0 / 0, Jaipur Weaves 192 / 4.2 → 0 / 0, Mumbai Linen House 147 / 4.5 → 0 / 0. Demo Textiles Co. (5) and Tirupur Textiles (4) already matched. After: `count_matches = true` on all 10 rows.
  - **Found and closed on the way: a vendor could write its own reputation.** `vprofiles_update` admits the owner and the guard trigger covered only `is_verified`. As demo-vendor, `reviews_count` 5 → 10004 and `rating_avg` → 5 was accepted, then reverted. The same migration makes `enforce_vendor_profile_admin_fields()` zero both on a signed-in INSERT and refuse a signed-in UPDATE of either. After: vendor → `REFUSED 42501`; super_admin → `REFUSED 42501`; an ordinary profile update → ok; a buyer editing their review still moves the aggregate (4.4 → 3.8 → 4.4 on revert). Logged in `securityflags.md` as Fixed.
  - **No fallback numbers on screen.** `VendorProfile.tsx` fell back, for a vendor with no reviews, to the seeded aggregate, then to 4.5 / 26 and a made-up 90/5/0/0/15 breakdown. It now shows "–/5" and "No reviews yet". The New Arrivals vendor tiles show a star only when the vendor has reviews, since "★ 0.0" reads as a bad rating rather than none; the vendor's own MyStore chip shows "–". Browser check: Lucknow → `Reviews and Ratings –/5 No reviews yet`, and "4,800" appears nowhere on the page; Demo Textiles → `4.4/5 Reviewed by 5 Users 5 Star 60% 4 Star 20% 3 Star 20%`; 36 New Arrivals vendor links, none showing "0.0". Screenshots: `screenshots/mp8-p2-vendor-reviews-*.png`.
  - **Logged, not fixed, on Mitra's decision:** the same vendor page invents a missing owner name, phone number, email, website, address, GSTIN and PAN; the product-level counters are vendor-writable. Both are in `securityflags.md` as Open.
  - `npm run typecheck` → 0 errors; `npx eslint .` → `✖ 22 problems (5 errors, 17 warnings)`, unchanged.
- 2026-09-11 (found during Master Prompt 8, Phase 2): **Every vendor profile page was blank on the live site.** `/vendor/:id` read `vendor.capacity.length` before the profile query had resolved. `vendor` is `undefined` on that first render, so the TypeError unmounted the whole page. It has been broken since `2279630` (2026-09-09). Reproduced on the live deployment (`/vendor/a0000005-…`: body text length 0, `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'capacity')`) and on the dev server. Fix: guard the block with `vendor &&`. After: 0 page errors, and the page renders for Lucknow Chikankari Co. and Demo Textiles Co.
- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 8 · Phase 1): **The exposed demo passwords are rotated and refused; no build can contain one; no test credential lives in source.** Closes the Critical flag logged below (`securityflags.md` → Fixed).
  - **Rotation first, before any code.** demo-admin, demo-buyer, demo-vendor, plus the throwaway `zz-mp4-vendor`, `zz-mp5-link` and three `zz-test-vendor-*` accounts, whose password was in the same public repo. New passwords were generated and bcrypt-hashed locally, and only the hash was written to `auth.users`. In the same transaction each account's sessions were deleted and its refresh tokens revoked; a password change alone would not have ended a session someone already held, and demo-admin had one. Password grant against `/auth/v1/token` for every demo account: old password → `HTTP 400 {"code":400,"error_code":"invalid_credentials","msg":"Invalid login credentials"}`, new password → `HTTP 200`.
  - **Mitra's decisions, asked rather than assumed:** demo-admin keeps `super_admin`; demo-buyer and demo-vendor are rotated too.
  - **Out of every build.** `vite.config.ts` defines `__DEMO_PASSWORDS__` from `.env` only for the dev server (`command === "serve"`) and as `null` for every build, `build:dev` included; `DEMO_ACCOUNTS` in `src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx` is `null` without it, and `DevAccountSwitcher` renders nothing. After `npm run build`, `grep -rlF` over `dist/` finds 0 files containing the old password, 0 containing any new one, and 0 containing the demo-admin email. The dev server still hands the switcher its passwords (through `/@vite/env`).
  - **From the environment.** New `scripts/lib/test-credentials.mjs` (and a copy in Cosora-Admin) reads `process.env`, then `.env`. Scripts call `credential()`, which throws with the variable's name. Specs call `optionalCredential()` and `test.skip` without it, because a throw while Playwright collects files would abort the whole run. New `.env.example` lists the seven names; `.github/workflows/e2e.yml` maps them from repository secrets, which a human still has to create. 24 files here. The prompt counted 17; the sweep also found the throwaway-vendor password in 7 test/script files and in `screenshots/zz-test-vendor.txt`, which `vendor-signup.spec.ts` wrote to on every run and no longer does. `git grep -lF` for each of the three old values → 0 files, in both repositories.
  - **Verified:** `npm run typecheck` → 0 errors; `npx eslint .` → `✖ 22 problems (5 errors, 17 warnings)`, the unchanged baseline; `node --check` passes on every script in both repos; both helpers sign in as each demo account and both throwaway vendors; `vendor-my-store.spec.ts` + `vendor-analytics.spec.ts` → 12 passed, 1 failed. The failure is an old assertion expecting an "Aadhaar" row on `/kyc`, which was removed when Aadhaar collection was deferred; it is unrelated to credentials and fixed in Phase 3.
  - **Not undone, and cannot be:** the old values stay in public git history and in bundles already deployed. Rotation is what makes them worthless.
- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 7, buyer-trust thread · security finding): **A super_admin credential is served to every visitor of the production site.** Logged in `securityflags.md` as Critical / Open; no code changed. `DEMO_ACCOUNTS` in `src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx` holds the shared demo password for `demo-admin@cosora.dev` — a `super_admin` — and is not gated on `import.meta.env.DEV`. `DevAccountSwitcher` correctly renders nothing in production, but the constant it imports still ships: the bundle served by the live deployment on 2026-09-11 contains the admin email and password as a string literal. The same password is hardcoded in 16 other tracked files, and this repository is public. The fix is rotation, and it needs a human: it breaks every script and spec that hardcodes the password. Found because the Bunny spec signs in as demo-admin — which also means an earlier report in this thread ("no admin credentials in this environment") was wrong; they were in the repository the whole time.

- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 7, buyer-trust thread · Phase 5): **The Bunny T8.1 "regression" was not Bunny's fault. Three real defects in the spec, fixed; the suite passes 6/6.** Changed: `tests/video-closeups-bunny.spec.ts`.
  - **Checked against Bunny, not assumed.** The exact video in the failure message, `7151edac…`, reads today (via the read-only `bunny-reconcile` edge function) as **status 4, `encodeProgress` 100, `hasMP4Fallback: true`, renditions 480p/360p/240p**. It was not stuck, failed or blocked — it finished encoding *after* the spec's 6-minute window. Bunny's API reference defines the status enum as 0 Created · 1 Uploaded · 2 Processing · 3 Transcoding · 4 Finished · 5 Error · 6 UploadFailed · 7 JitSegmenting · 8 JitPlaylistsCreated, so "status=2" in the failure meant Processing. MP4 Fallback is still on (both library videos report it), and nothing points to a quota or billing problem: the uploads succeeded and encoded.
  - **Defect 1 — the spec misread the enum.** Its comment said "3 = Finished, 4 = Resolution finished" and it accepted either. 3 is *Transcoding*, so the spec could proceed while the renditions it was about to play were still being built. Only 4 counts as done now, and 5 (Error) or 6 (UploadFailed) fail immediately instead of waiting out the timeout.
  - **Defect 2 — every slow encode leaked a paid asset.** The row was inserted *after* the encode wait, and `afterAll` only cleans up `if (rowId)`, so a transcode that outlasted the window left a ~21 MB video at Bunny with no row and nothing able to find it. `bunny-reconcile` lists **two such orphans**, `9f07b47a…` (2026-09-09) and `7151edac…`, one per failed run. The row is now inserted as soon as the upload lands — which is also what `UploadVideo.tsx` does in the real product — so `afterAll` can always delete the asset through `bunny-delete-video`. **The two existing orphans were left alone:** deleting assets from a billed third-party library is a human's call, and reconcile's own note says to review orphans by hand.
  - **Defect 3 — tests that never touch Bunny were blocked by it.** T8.1 checks that a renamed QuickTime file is rejected *client-side, before any upload*, and T8.1b is its positive control; neither uploads anything. Both sat behind a shared `beforeAll` that uploaded a real video and waited on its transcode, so a slow Bunny failed them and serially skipped T8.2–T8.5 as well. The Bunny arrangement is now a memoised helper that only T8.2–T8.5 await; `beforeAll` keeps only fixtures and sign-ins.
  - **Verified:** T8.1 + T8.1b alone, 2/2 in 14 s with zero Bunny calls; the full suite, **6/6 in 1.8 min**; `bunny-reconcile` afterwards lists the same two videos as before the run, with `dbRowCount: 0` — the run's own upload was cleaned up. This run encoded quickly, so it exercised the normal path; the slow-encode path is covered by the ordering change, not by a live timeout.

The seed and drop scripts already exist in Cosora-Admin (`scripts/seed-test-admins.sql` + `drop-test-admins.sql`; `scripts/seed-chat-fixtures.sql` + `drop-chat-fixtures.sql`), and both seeds insert into `auth.users`, so they must run as the service role or in the SQL editor. This session had neither: the Supabase MCP server was disconnected before it started, and there is no service-role key in the repo, by design. No fixtures were created, so there was nothing to drop. **To run it:** in the Supabase SQL editor run `seed-test-admins.sql` and `seed-chat-fixtures.sql`; run the two specs; then run `drop-chat-fixtures.sql` and `drop-test-admins.sql`, and confirm each script's closing select returns `leftover_fixture_users = 0` and `leftover_users = 0` — in the same session, every time.

The copy no longer implies otherwise: "NEW TREND INSIGHTS — Discover Trending Arrivals for You" → "CURATED TREND PICKS — Editor-picked looks"; "Hot Trends, Styled for You" → "Curated looks"; "Hot Keywords" → "Suggested searches"; "Also Trending" → "Explore More"; the "Accessories/Trending" group → "Accessories". A real trends pipeline is logged in `ToDo.md`.

And the real cards on this page show the seeded `rating_avg` and `sold_count` values described in Phase 1, e.g. "800+ sold" on a listing with no sales data behind it.

- 2026-09-11 (Master Prompt 7, buyer-trust thread · Phase 1): **Every product page was a real listing layered over a fake one. `/product/:id` now renders only the database row.** Changed: `src/pages/ProductDetail.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/products.ts`. New: `tests/mp7-product-detail-real-data.spec.ts`.
  - **The bug was not what it looked like from outside.** The page did read the real row (`useProductById`) — then built `{ ...mockChinos, ...realFields }`, so every field the row did not override was inherited from a hardcoded "Premium Cotton Chinos" template. Measured against all 26 live listings (anon key, `status='live'`): **all 26** showed GOTS and OEKO-TEX certification as fact, "Usually responds within 4 hours" and product code `TF-MDS-0412`; **23** showed four invented named reviewers and a 100-review breakdown, because they have no `product_reviews` rows; **20** showed the chinos' "100% organic cotton" description, because they have none of their own. A listing without images would have borrowed the chinos' photos and a Google sample MP4. That is worse than a wholly fake page: it was convincingly real.
  - **A nonexistent id did NOT render the chinos** — the not-found guard already existed. But it collapsed *error* and *not-found* into one state (`isError || !row`), so a network failure passed for a missing product.
  - **Now:** a real-only view model (`toViewModel`); `PRODUCTS`, `getDefaultProduct` and the mock block are deleted, not kept as a fallback. Fields with no real column are gone from the UI — certifications, response time, product code, sold count. Absent data says so: "Price on request" instead of "₹0", "No photos yet", "Not specified by the vendor", "The vendor hasn't added a description", "Country of origin not specified". `products.unit` is now selected and used: "/ Piece" was hardcoded on every listing, and seeded MOQs rendered as "50 pieces Pieces". Error and not-found are distinct states, and a malformed id short-circuits to not-found before any request, because PostgREST errors on a non-uuid. The selected colour was seeded from the template on first render — before the row arrived — so real products showed "Beige" selected.
  - **Ratings come from rows, never the denormalised columns.** Against the live data, `products.reviews_count` disagrees with the real `product_reviews` count on **23 of 26** listings ("Hand-Embroidered Kurta" claims 480 and has none), and `vendor_profiles.reviews_count` is seed on at least one vendor ("Lucknow Chikankari Co." claims 4,800 with zero rows in `reviews`). Where real reviews exist the columns agree (4.25 → 4.3), so something maintains them — it just never reset the seed values. `products.sold_count` has **no writer** in either repo and there is no orders table. The page derives the product rating from `product_reviews` and the vendor rating from `reviews`, and shows nothing when there are none.
  - **Verified in a real browser, 5/5:** two live products render their own name, price, MOQ, vendor and category, and differ from each other; the one with 4 real reviews shows them, with its vendor's real 5; the one with none says "No ratings yet" and "No vendor reviews yet" (not 4,800); a nonexistent uuid and a malformed id both render "Product not found", never "Couldn't load"; no template string, picsum image or sample video anywhere. Screenshots: `screenshots/mp7-product-*.png`.
  - **Found, not fixed:** every listing card elsewhere in the app reads `rating_avg` and `sold_count`, so the seeded ratings and "800+ sold" figures still show on New Arrivals, Search, For You and Trends cards. On this page, the vendor card's Follow toggle is local state only (it resets on reload; `useFollowing` exists and is not wired), and "Add Fabric", "Download PDF", "Translate", the review "Helpful?" counter and the ⋮ menu do nothing.
- 2026-09-11: **x-forwarded-for settled empirically on this deployment; image-search check script given the repo's service-key cleanup pattern; two stale docs corrected.** The previous session's claim that "the client IP can't be faked here" rested on one anecdote. It is now replaced by a measured result, and the result holds, but for a better reason, with limits spelled out. Files: `supabase/functions/image-search/index.ts` (**comment only; parsing unchanged**; deployed **v7**), `scripts/image-search-check.mjs`, and docs `claude.md`, `securityflags.md`, `technicalimplementation.md`, `test.md`. **Not touched:** embed-query's code, `embed_query_rate_check`, the rate-limit trade-off (Step 5: noted, left as is).
  - **The test.** A temporary build of image-search (**v6**) answered a one-off nonce by echoing its IP-related headers, returning before the limiter and before any OpenAI call. The client's real public IPv4 was confirmed by three independent outside services. Six cases, 3 requests each, plus a curl repeat of the forged pair:
    - (a) no forged header;
    - (b) forged `x-forwarded-for: 203.0.113.7`;
    - (c) forged `203.0.113.7, 198.51.100.9`;
    - (d) a forged non-IP token;
    - (e) a forged IPv6 `2001:db8::1`;
    - (f) a forged `x-real-ip` only.
  - **Observed, all 19 requests:** the header that reached the function was exactly `<real>,<real>, <upstream proxy>`. **No forged value appeared in any position**, `x-real-ip` was stripped entirely, and `cf-connecting-ip` carried the real address. The header is rebuilt at the edge; it is neither appended to nor passed through. So `.split(",")[0]` is correct on this deployment and was **kept**. The earlier anecdote could not have shown this: its forged value was not a valid IP, so it could equally have been a gateway dropping malformed input.
  - **The finding that matters most: the obvious "fix" would have been a bug.** The LAST entry is an upstream proxy address that changed on every request (`13.248.105.16`–`.46`). Switching to "take the last entry", the usual `x-forwarded-for` hardening advice and one of the brief's proposed fixes, would have put every buyer into a handful of shared rotating proxy buckets. The comment next to the parsing line now records precisely what was tested and warns against that change. **Limits of the claim:** one date, one IPv4 client, observed platform behaviour, not a Supabase guarantee.
  - **Probe removed and confirmed gone.** v7 = the v5 logic plus the new comments; the old nonce now gets `400 no_image`. `node scripts/image-search-check.mjs` **18/18 against v7**; the per-IP loop tripped on call 9, exactly the prediction from a clean bucket, so v7 keys the bucket on the real address.
  - **Check script:** it now reads credentials exactly as `migrate-kyc-to-private-bucket.mjs` does: the repo-root `.env` (no longer cwd-relative), with a `process.env` override for `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`. With the key, it snapshots the `img:ip:` / `img:user:` rows before the run, then deletes every row the run created, resets any pre-existing row to its pre-run count (so it removes exactly its own increments), and confirms both from a fresh read.

So the **service-key path is written but has NOT been exercised live**. Today's run took the print-SQL fallback, and that SQL was run via MCP: **0** of the run's rows remained, and **0** `img:ip:` / `img:user:` rows exist in total.

  - **Stale docs fixed.**
    - `technicalimplementation.md`: the embed-query line still said "no rate limit". It now describes `embed_query_rate_check` (30/IP/5 min, 10,000/hour global, cache-miss only, fails open) and cross-references the Master Prompt 6 entry and image-search's reuse. The 2026-09-10 paragraph that rested on the anecdote is replaced with the full test table.

  - **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` exit 0 and `-p tsconfig.app.json` 0 errors; `get_advisors(security)` **4 / 1 / 30 / 43 / 1**, identical to the recorded baseline (no DDL this session).
- 2026-09-10: **Photo search now refuses non-product images and is rate limited. Closes the two items the previous photo-search entry flagged and deliberately deferred.** Both are logged and closed in `documentation/securityflags.md`: "image-search has no rate limit" (Medium) and "No rejection path for non-product images" (Low). Files: new migration `supabase/migrations/20260910190000_image_search_rate_limit.sql` (**applied**; recorded in the database as version `20260910132753`), `supabase/functions/image-search/index.ts` (**deployed as v5**, `verify_jwt` still on), `src/pages/Search.tsx`, `scripts/image-search-check.mjs`. **Not touched:** `embed-query`, `embed_query_rate_check`, embed-query's `global` / `ip:` rows, and the nightly prune job. The photo → text query → normal catalogue search design and the `gpt-4o-mini` model are unchanged.
  - **Rate limit: a copy of a proven pattern, not a new one.** `image_search_rate_check(p_ip, p_user_id, p_ip_limit 10, p_ip_window_secs 600, p_global_limit 300, p_global_window_secs 3600)` was written after reading `embed_query_rate_check`'s live body with `pg_get_functiondef`. It keeps the same fixed-window UPSERT, the same global-before-per-IP ordering, and the same SECURITY DEFINER + `search_path`. It **reuses `embed_query_rate_limit`** with namespaced keys (`img:global`, `img:ip:<addr>`, `img:user:<jwt sub>`), so the two features' budgets cannot collide. The user bucket is new and is the reason the signature takes `p_user_id`: `x-forwarded-for` is client-supplied in principle, but a JWT `sub` is signed. **Unlike embed-query, the check runs on every call that would reach OpenAI.** There is no cache in front of a vision call, so there is no cheap path to exempt. On `false` the function returns `{ error: "rate_limited" }` at 200. **It fails open** if the RPC errors: that lives in the TypeScript `catch`, exactly as in embed-query, whose SQL function also has no exception handler (the brief's "fails OPEN on any error" describes the edge function, not the SQL). The `sub` is decoded from the JWT payload without re-verifying the signature, because `verify_jwt` has already rejected any bad token before the handler runs. Anon-key tokens have no `sub`, so anon callers are bounded by the global and IP buckets only.
  - **Grants done the way this project learned the hard way.** EXECUTE is revoked from `public, anon, authenticated` (both kinds of grant, per claude.md) and granted to `service_role`. Checked, not assumed: `has_function_privilege` returns anon **false**, authenticated **false**, service_role **true**, and the ACL is `postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres`, identical in shape to `embed_query_rate_check`. **`get_advisors(security)` after the DDL: unchanged.** `rls_enabled_no_policy` 4 (`embed_query_rate_limit` was already one of them), anon-executable SECURITY DEFINER **30 → 30**, authenticated **43 → 43**, and the new function appears in no list.
  - **Structured Outputs, confirmed against the live API rather than assumed.** The vision request now sends `response_format: json_schema` (`image_search_result`, `strict: true`; `is_apparel_or_textile: boolean`, `query: string | null`, both required, `additionalProperties: false`). The prompt was rewritten to describe the item ONLY when the photo clearly shows an apparel, fabric, trim, accessory or other textile/fashion product, and otherwise to return false/null ("do not guess"). The function now parses JSON instead of a raw string: a false verdict, a null query or a safety `refusal` return `{ error: "no_match" }`, and unparseable content returns `bad_model_output`. `max_tokens` went 40 → 80 because the JSON envelope adds ~15 tokens and a truncated object cannot parse. There is no local OpenAI key, so the confirmation came from the first live calls after deploying v5. The function passes OpenAI's error text through as `vision_failed`, so an unsupported `response_format` would have shown up immediately. **Live results:**
    - the real garment fixture → `{"query":"men white t-shirt"}`, **no regression**;
    - a 256×256 solid-colour PNG generated byte by byte in Node → `{"error":"no_match"}`, where the old prompt produced "men blue denim jacket".
  - **Search.tsx: every code has its own branch.** `rate_limited` → "Too many photo searches / Try again in a few minutes, or search by text." `no_match` → the existing "Couldn't recognise that image / Try another photo or search by text.", now referenced explicitly for that code. **The fallback for any other code** (vision_failed, request_failed, bad_model_output, or one added later) **is now "Image search unavailable / Please try again."** instead of the recognition copy, so a service failure no longer tells the buyer their photo was the problem.
  - **Found while testing: this project's gateway sets `x-forwarded-for` itself.** An anon call that sent `x-forwarded-for: zz-xffprobe-…` was counted under this machine's real address, and no `img:ip:zz-xffprobe-…` row was ever created. So the per-IP bucket is harder to evade here than the code comment (which still treats it as spoofable, the safe reading) assumes. It also means a test cannot use a throwaway IP bucket: the loop exhausts the real one, and cleanup has to remove it.
  - **Verified, four ways:**
    - **Database layer**, in a self-rolling-back `DO` block (it RAISEs at the end, so nothing persisted): per-IP limit 3 → calls 1–3 true, call 4 **false**; same user across 4 *rotating* IPs → call 4 **false**, so the user bucket bounds IP rotation; anon across 4 rotating IPs → all true and **0** user rows created; global limit 1 → **false**, and the IP row was **not** created, so the global check really runs first; empty IP → `img:ip:unknown`; embed-query's own rows **byte-identical** before and after.
    - **`node scripts/image-search-check.mjs` — 18/18 live** (was 13; the 5 new checks are listed in `test.md`). The per-IP loop, run as anon so demo-buyer's user bucket is not exhausted, went `no_match` ×8, then **`rate_limited` on call 9**. That is exactly the predicted call: 2 calls already spent in steps 2 and 4, plus 9, is 11 > 10. The throttled reply came back in 514 ms against ~1.6 s for a real vision call, consistent with no OpenAI call being made.
    - **Real browser, real `/search` page** (Playwright, scratch script):
      - **LIVE** while this IP's budget was genuinely spent: the cooldown toast shows, neither other copy shows, and no search runs (8/8).
      - **LIVE** solid square: the recognition toast, no search (6/6).
      - **MOCKED** `{"error":"some_future_code"}`, because the live function cannot be made to emit an unknown code: the generic toast, never the recognition copy (5/5).
      - **LIVE** garment: navigates to `/search/results?q=men%20white%20cotton%20t-shirt`.
      - No page errors in either pass.
    - **`npx tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` exit 0**; `-p tsconfig.app.json` **0 errors**.
  - **Synthetic limiter rows cleaned up and confirmed by count.** `embed_query_rate_limit` has RLS on and no policies, and there is no service-role key in this environment, so the script cannot clear its own rows. With `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` set it does, following `migrate-kyc-to-private-bucket.mjs`'s optional-key precedent; without it, it prints the exact SQL. Run here via MCP, in three rounds:
    - before the script, the Step 3 probe rows: 2 deleted;
    - after the script and the throttled browser pass: `img:ip:136.233.9.123` (count **12** = 2 buyer + 9 loop + 1 browser) and demo-buyer's `img:user:` row (count 2) deleted, then `count(*)` = **0**;
    - after the final browser pass: its one `img:ip:` row deleted, then **0** `img:ip:` / `img:user:` rows remaining.
    The shared **`img:global` counter was deliberately left in place**: it is production state. It reads **17**, every limiter call this session made since the row was first created, against a 300/hour ceiling, and that count expires with its window.
  - **Accepted trade-off, recorded in securityflags.md:** the global bucket is shared, so one abuser burning 300 calls in an hour turns photo search off for everyone until the window rolls. Same reasoning as embed-query's global bucket: the alternative is an unbounded bill.
- 2026-09-10 (Master Prompt 8): **Recently Viewed no longer fabricates browsing history, and the alert-routing decision is logged for a human instead of being made in code.** Commits `9f5b9c6` (fix + verification script) and `b3e0289` (ToDo), both pushed; `git ls-remote` returned `b3e0289` as GitHub's `refs/heads/main`.
  - **What was wrong:** `recentlyViewedStore.ts` returned `SEED` — six invented products, `rv1`..`rv6` — whenever localStorage was empty: every first-time visitor, every cleared browser, and every sign-out (`setRecentUser(null)` reloads through `load()`). Every card linked to `/product/rv1`..`rv6` and every Chat / CALL NOW button to vendors `v-rv1`..`v-rv6`. None of them exist.
  - **It persisted itself.** `recordView()` builds `[thisView, ...items]`, and on a first visit `items` *was* the seed — so a signed-out buyer's first real product view wrote all six fakes into localStorage alongside it. Fixing `load()` alone would have left every such browser showing them indefinitely. `load()` now drops any stored row whose id is not a UUID and rewrites storage; ProductDetail records a view only once its DB row has loaded, so every real entry has one.
  - **A trap in the obvious fix, avoided:** `UUID_RE` was declared below `let items = load()`. `load()` runs at module init, so calling `isUuid()` from it would have hit the `const`'s temporal dead zone and thrown on import. Moved above it.
  - **The DB was already authoritative for signed-in buyers — once hydrated.** The gap was the window before: with the seed gone, a signed-in buyer's real history would have flashed "No recently viewed products" while it loaded. The store now exports hydrating/owner state and the page renders loading, empty and populated as three distinct states, matching SearchResults and ForYou. `hydrateRecent()` also stopped swallowing query errors — a failed fetch used to render as an empty history.

Storage holding legacy rv rows plus one real row → rv rows gone, real row kept, storage rewritten to the one real id. Signed-out view of a real product → exactly that product, stored by UUID. Signed in → DB-backed history where every card is one of the buyer's own rows, and a 10 ms poll from first paint proved the empty state **never** flashed while loading. 5 of the demo buyer's 6 DB rows render: the sixth product is no longer visible to the buyer under RLS and is correctly left out rather than shown as a dead link. The script judges on hrefs and stored ids, not names — "Premium Cotton Polo T-Shirt" is both a seed name and a real listing's name.

- 2026-09-10: **Photo search verified working END TO END for the first time — real key, real photo, real query, real results. Closes the gap the 2026-07-04 entry left open.** That entry verified the `image-search` function was "deployed + reachable", which with no key meant it answered `{ error: "not_configured" }`. Whether a photo produced a query, and whether that query then ran through catalogue search, was never checked. For two months both photo buttons toasted "isn't set up yet" and nothing noticed. Files: `src/pages/Search.tsx`, new `scripts/image-search-check.mjs`, new `scripts/fixtures/polo-tshirt-listing.jpg`. The function itself (version 4) and the photo → short query → normal catalogue search design are **unchanged**, and so is the model (`gpt-4o-mini`).
  - **`OPENAI_API_KEY` was added to the project's edge-function secrets — confirmed live before anything was built on it.** A direct POST of a real catalogue photo (the "Premium Cotton Polo T-Shirt" listing image, 20 KB) to the deployed function returned **HTTP 200 `{"query":"men white t-shirt"}` in 3.7 s**, not `not_configured`.
  - **Fix: the raw camera file is no longer uploaded as-is.** `fileToDataUrl` used to base64 the original `File` untouched, so a phone photo went up as a multi-MB, 4000px data URL over a mobile connection. That was pure waste: the function calls the vision model at `detail:"low"`, so OpenAI downsamples to 512px on arrival anyway. New `compressForUpload()` in `Search.tsx` draws the image onto a canvas at a **1024px longest edge, never upscaling**, and re-encodes it as **JPEG q0.8**. An image already ≤1024px is sent **byte-for-byte untouched**, not recompressed. Transparent PNGs get a white background, because JPEG has no alpha and they would otherwise arrive black. If the browser can't decode the file (e.g. HEIC outside Safari), the original bytes go up, exactly as before, so this can never break an upload that used to work. The preview overlay still shows the original, and toasts, error handling and the `{ image, mimeType }` request shape are unchanged.
  - **Compression verified in a real browser against the real `/search` page** (Playwright; drove the actual gallery input and captured the outgoing `image-search` request body). **30/30 checks, 0 page errors:**

    | Input | Sent | Size |
    |---|---|---|
    | phone-camera original 3024×4032 JPEG | 768×1024 JPEG | 767,695 → **44,242 bytes (6%)** |
    | the only real photo in Storage, 1200×1600 JPEG | 768×1024 JPEG | 405,503 → 253,977 (63%) |
    | 500×650 listing photo (already small) | **byte-identical to the original**, `image/jpeg` kept | 38,622 → 38,622 |
    | transparent 1600×1200 PNG | 1024×768 JPEG, transparent corner = `rgba(255,255,255,255)` | 41,923 → 5,569 |

    For every photo, the preview overlay still showed and the page still navigated to `/search/results?q=…` with the model's query.
  - **New `node scripts/image-search-check.mjs` — the check that has been missing since July. 13/13 against the live project.** Written in the house style of `engagement-events-check.mjs`: signs in as `demo-buyer`, asserts outcomes, exits non-zero on failure. It **reuses rather than reimplements** the search path: it esbuild-bundles the app's own `src/lib/supabase.ts` client and `fetchSearch` out of `src/lib/queries/search.ts`, the exact function `useProductSearch` runs on `/search/results` (`search_products` → `embed-query` warm-up → hydrate → re-rank). `fetchSearch` is module-private, so a load-time esbuild plugin appends `export { fetchSearch }` in memory and the source file is not touched. Asserts: (1) an empty body → **400 `no_image`**, at no OpenAI cost; (2) the fixture photo → a non-empty `query` that honours the function's own contract: **3–6 lowercase words, no punctuation**. A hyphen inside a word is allowed, since the function's own prompt example is `t-shirt`. (3) That query through `fetchSearch` → resolves and returns an array. **Empty counts as a pass**, because the check proves the pipeline runs, not that one photo has a catalogue match. **Result of the verifying run:** query `"white cotton t-shirt"` (1.7 s) → **26 results, semantic + keyword**, led by *Premium Cotton Polo T-Shirt*, the very listing the photo came from. Costs one `gpt-4o-mini` call per run, plus at most one embedding for a never-before-seen query. Writes nothing to the database.
  - **Fixture provenance, and a deviation from the plan.** The brief was to pull a real garment photo already in Supabase Storage. **There isn't one.** All 26 live listings' images are Unsplash-hosted. Every public Storage object is a 70-byte placeholder PNG left by test runs except exactly one real photo, and that belongs to a **rejected "Button" listing** (category Buttons, a trim, not a garment or fabric) owned by a real vendor. That was not committed to git. The fixture is instead the exact bytes the live catalogue serves for the Premium Cotton Polo T-Shirt listing (500×650, 38.6 KB). Because it is ≤1024px, `compressForUpload()` passes it through untouched, so the script sends exactly what a browser sends for that file.
  - **Observed, NOT fixed (out of scope, reported rather than silently dropped):**
    - **The prompt always produces a garment, even for a non-garment image.** A flat blue rectangle came back as `"men blue denim jacket"` and ran a search. Nothing in the function returns a "not apparel" verdict, so an irrelevant photo silently produces plausible-looking results instead of the "Couldn't recognise that image" toast.
    - **`image-search` is callable by any holder of the public anon key and costs one vision call per request, with no rate limit.** This is the same exposure shape as `embed-query` (see "Watch the spend" in `claude.md`), and it is now live because the key exists. Rate limiting is deliberately a separate, undecided piece of work.
  - **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` **exit 0**, and `npx tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck -p tsconfig.app.json` **0 errors** (the bare form compiles nothing in this repo, so the app-tsconfig run is the one that counts); `node scripts/image-search-check.mjs` **13/13**; browser compression check **30/30**.

They were. `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` (which queries GitHub live, not a cached ref) returned `d3b5ccf`, and both fixes landed in `ecea749` — confirmed an **ancestor of origin/main** via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, present on `main` only, with a clean working tree and nothing unpushed. On `origin/main` the only occurrences of `PRODUCT_POOL` and `preferredVideoCategoryNames` are the REMOVED comments; `usePreferredVideoCategoryNames()` resolves through `pref_category_map` and both callers import it. Nothing was redone. **Lesson for future prompts: `git status -sb` compares against a LOCAL `origin/main` ref that is only as fresh as the last fetch — `git ls-remote` is the only command here that actually asks GitHub.**

Verified both directions: with inventory (one ad temporarily un-expired, then reverted, all three confirmed back to `unexpired=false`) the block renders the real product name and logs exactly one `ad_impression`; a click logs `ad_click` and lands on `/product/36f94dd6-…`, the ad's real `product_id` — correct, because placement `openListing,featuredProduct` is not a profile goal. With no inventory the heading is absent and no `/product/rv*` request is ever made.
  - **BUG (real, user-visible, found while verifying the above): the For You feed never showed more than 8 of 26 products.** The infinite-scroll `IntersectionObserver` was never attached for anyone who completed onboarding in-session. The onboarding gate is an **early return**, so while it shows, the sentinel is not in the DOM and `loadMoreRef.current` is null — the effect bails. On "Skip for now" the feed mounts and the sentinel appears, but the catalogue has usually already loaded by then, so `visible.length` and `filtered.length` are **unchanged** and the effect never re-runs. Buyers saw "Scroll for more" that never loaded more, for the rest of the session. **Diagnosed rather than guessed:** instrumenting the callback produced **zero** invocations while a hand-attached `IntersectionObserver` on the same node fired normally, which ruled out the observer and pointed at the attach step; confirmed pre-existing by stashing the change and reproducing on `d3b5ccf`. Fix is one dependency — `prefs.hasCompleted`. Measured 8 cards -> **all 26, "You're all caught up"**.

Separately, the alerts **were** already reachable, in the wrong app: the buyer/vendor `/notifications` page selects every kind without filtering, its RLS is `profile_id = auth.uid()`, and unknown kinds fall through to `FALLBACK_META` and render. Admins do not work there. Zero `kind='system'` rows exist today, which is correct rather than a gap — the notifier fires only on a **transition** into a non-OK state and the pipeline has stayed healthy.

The log table stays **service_role-only with RLS on and no policies** — it is not widened to `authenticated` just to make a page possible. Verified the guard **raises 42501** for an unauthenticated caller rather than returning rows. `SECTION_READ` mirrors the RPC's guard exactly, because listing a role the database refuses would put a nav entry in front of a page that can only error.
  - **A way out of the app, opt-in and inert by default.** `notify_embedding_alert_webhook()` — a fire-and-forget `pg_net` POST to the Vault secret `embedding_alert_webhook_url`, **CRITICAL only**. There is no email sender, Slack app or webhook integration anywhere in this project and building one speculatively was out of scope, so this is the cheapest thing that actually leaves the building: verified it returns `false` with no call and no error while the secret is absent, and returns `false` for WARN. An admin enables it later with any URL that accepts a JSON POST — Slack, Discord, PagerDuty Events v2, an internal receiver — **with no redeploy**. WARN deliberately stays in-app: paging on the state a transient backlog produces is how an alert channel gets muted. Wrapped in an exception handler on top of pg_net's async return, so a dead webhook can never slow or break the health check watching it.
