    | `for_you_products` | 4.9 ms | **5.000 ms** | opaque (plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER) |

  - **The single most important line in the whole re-run:** `match_vendor_rfqs` now shows `SubPlan 1 -> Aggregate (loops=1)`. Before MP6's `with v as materialized` fix it was `loops=2003` at 1,991 ms with 1,155,858 buffers; it is now **loops=1, 24,727 buffers, 36.5 ms**. That is the fix holding, verified from a plan rather than from a claim.
  - **Also confirmed at 10k scale:** `zzzznotathing` still returns **0 rows**, so Master Prompt 5's `max_distance = 0.80` — calibrated on 26 products — continues to hold two orders of magnitude up.
  - **Cleanup verified:** back to exactly 33 products / 1 video / 4 RFQs / 10 vendors, **0** synthetic rows in every table, 0 synthetic `auth.users`, 0 scaffold tables, queue depth 0. Vendor `catalog_embedding`s recomputed from the real catalogue (synthetic listings had been averaged in) and RFQ matching re-verified to reproduce the exact pre-test numbers (`Fleece Hoodies` -> Delhi Fashion Hub 0.416/true/0.591; `Premium Cotton T-Shirts` -> Tirupur Textiles 0.618/true/0.733).
  - **Found but NOT fixed, reported rather than silently skipped:** `src/lib/recentlyViewedStore.ts` seeds **6 fabricated products** (`rv1`..`rv6` — "Premium Cotton Polo T-Shirt / Tirupur Textiles", "Kids Cotton Shorts / Gujarat Garments") **ungated in production** whenever localStorage is empty, pointing at the same dead `/product/rv*` routes this prompt just removed from ForYou. Same class of violation, different feature (Recently Viewed) with its own store and page, so it needs its own empty state rather than being folded into an ad-strip fix.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` **0 errors** (both repos); eslint unchanged from baseline; `vite build` green in both; `scripts/search-smoke.mjs` **10/10**; ForYou browser checks **5/5 with inventory** and **5/5 without**; `get_advisors(security)` after DDL — anon-executable SECURITY DEFINER count **unchanged at 30**, authenticated +1 (the intended guarded admin read path); `get_advisors(performance)` introduced nothing new.
- 2026-09-10 (Master Prompt 6): **Closed the four known gaps, then stress-tested the whole vector stack at 10k products / 2.5k videos / 2k RFQs / 510 vendors. Found two severe scaling defects that were invisible at production volume — one made a vendor's lead feed take 1.6 SECONDS, the other put up to 1.4s of synchronous work inside every product write.** New migrations `20260909193602`, `20260909193630`, `20260910110000`, `20260910120000`, `20260910130000`, `20260910140000`, `20260910150000`, `20260910160000`, `20260910170000` (all **applied**); both edge functions redeployed; `src/pages/ForYou.tsx`, `src/pages/NewArrivals.tsx`, `src/pages/VideoCloseUpsPage.tsx`, `src/lib/buyerCategories.ts`, `src/lib/queries/forYou.ts`.

  **PART 1 — the four known gaps.**
  - **The `reject_vendor_content` grant "regression" was never a regression — it was a no-op from the day it was written, and so were two sibling revokes.** Migration `20260801100327` ran `revoke execute ... from public`. The usual suspect (a `DROP`+`CREATE` wiping grants) is provably not the cause: no migration ever dropped these functions, and `20260801102505` explicitly used `ALTER FUNCTION ... RENAME` to preserve the OID and ACL. The real cause is that Supabase registers `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS TO anon, authenticated, service_role` in `pg_default_acl` for BOTH the `postgres` and `supabase_admin` grantors, so every function born in `public` carries **explicit per-role grants**, and revoking the PUBLIC pseudo-role leaves them untouched. **Demonstrated, not inferred:** created a throwaway function, ran the identical revoke against it, and the ACL came back byte-identical. Fixed all five admin verbs (the three above plus `set_account_status`, `set_vendor_document_verified`); `authenticated` is deliberately KEPT because admins sign in and the real gate is each function's internal `is_admin()` guard. **`set_account_status` needed the exact inverse fix** — it had no `anon` grant at all and was exposed purely through PUBLIC, so `from anon` was the no-op there. Lesson worth keeping: PUBLIC and named-role grants are independent, either alone leaves a function callable, and a revoke running without error proves nothing. Anon-executable SECURITY DEFINER functions **35 -> 30**.
  - **`ForYou.tsx`'s 48 fabricated products deleted.** `PRODUCT_POOL` rendered whenever the catalogue was loading **or empty** — and `!live.length` is indistinguishable from a *failed fetch*, so a catalogue outage rendered a full page of plausible fake suppliers ("Artisan Weaves Co.", "SilkThread Mills", invented "5.6k enquiries") that a buyer could tap through to a dead product route. Loading / empty / populated are now three distinct states, and the empty state distinguishes an empty CATALOGUE from an over-narrow FILTER, because "reset your preferences" is useless advice for the first one. Verified in-browser past the onboarding gate: **26 real products, honest count line, zero fabricated suppliers, no console errors.**
  - **`preferredVideoCategoryNames()` was silently a no-op for 8 of 9 preferences.** It mapped preference ids onto hardcoded pre-2026-09-07 flat category names. Measured against the live tree: `tshirts` -> `"T-shirts/Tops"` where the real categories are `Men's T-Shirts / Unisex T-Shirts / Women's Tops`; same for shirts, dresses, bottomwear, kidswear, accessories. Every left-hand name matched **zero** live categories. Only `activewear` survived, by coincidence of naming. Replaced with `usePreferredVideoCategoryNames()` resolving through the `pref_category_map` table — the same source `buyer_cold_start_embedding()` and the For You feed already use — joined to `categories.name` because the video side matches on `product_videos.category` (a text copy of the name) rather than on `category_id`.
  - **Backfilled 3 of the 4 legacy July RFQs by hand.** `Premium Cotton T-Shirts` -> Unisex T-Shirts, `Fleece Hoodies` -> Winter Wear, `Fabrics — Linen` -> Woven Fabrics — all TREE children, since the same-named ROOT rows are dead legacy holding zero live products. `Quote request: Abhishek Mitra` **left null on purpose**: empty description, auto-generated title, no derivable signal. Cost **zero OpenAI calls** — queue depth 0 before and after, confirming `enqueue_rfq_embedding` still excludes `category_id`. Immediately visible in output: `Fleece Hoodies` now ranks two category-matched vendors ABOVE a higher-similarity one (0.416/0.391 with the boost beating 0.432 without).
  - **`products_embedding_idx` migration made replayable.** The file said `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, which every migration runner rejects inside its transaction (`25001`) — so a fresh rebuild from history would have **aborted there** rather than reproducing live state, the exact opposite of the file's purpose. It was also never registered in `schema_migrations`, so it sat waiting to break the next `db push`. CONCURRENTLY dropped (it buys nothing against the empty table a rebuild runs on) and the version registered.

  **PART 2 — scale hardening, tested against synthetic volume then fully cleaned up.**
  - **`match_vendor_rfqs` took 1,599 ms warm, and the cause was not what it looked like.** Not the vector maths and not the composite `0.7*similarity + 0.3*category_match` sort (a top-N heapsort over 2,003 rows is microseconds). `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` showed `SubPlan 1 -> Aggregate (loops=2003)`: the planner **inlined the single-row `v` CTE and re-ran the vendor's whole per-vendor category aggregate once for EVERY RFQ being scored** — 2,003 x 1,032 = ~2.07M product row reads for one call, and **1,135,701 of 1,155,858 buffers (98%)** in that subplan alone. Quadratic in exactly the two dimensions the marketplace grows in, worst for the vendor with the biggest catalogue. Fix is one word — `with v as materialized` — and it is load-bearing, not style: **loops 2003 -> 1, buffers 1,155,858 -> 20,724, 1,991 ms -> 38 ms.**
  - **`recompute_vendor_catalog_embedding` put 0.45-1.4 SECONDS inside every product write.** Its own 2026-09-07 migration comment said this was fine "at this volume" and named the trigger to watch: "a vendor ever carries thousands of live listings". Measured at ~1,000 live listings: **1,364.7 ms / 1,096.4 ms / 453.0 ms**, synchronous, inside the vendor's own transaction — their "save listing" request blocked on it, and a 500-product bulk import was O(N^2), roughly **8 minutes of pure trigger overhead**. Moved onto a queue table keyed on `vendor_id` (a TABLE, not pgmq, because **dedupe is the whole point** and it falls out of the primary key) drained by a new `vendor-catalog-recompute` cron. Dedupe proven live: 6 product writes for one vendor collapsed to exactly **1** pending row. Product writes now **5.2-8.4 ms**. Cost: `catalog_embedding` is eventually consistent by up to one tick — acceptable because it feeds ONLY RFQ<->vendor matching; a vendor's own listings still appear in buyer search immediately via a different column on a different path.
  - **The embedding pipeline's throughput ceiling was 20 jobs/minute and nothing else.** `BATCH = 20` per invocation x one cron tick per minute = 1,200/hour regardless of backlog. **Measured, not inferred:** 300 jobs enqueued 19:48:13 drained at exactly 20/tick, finishing ~20:03 — no faster with 300 waiting than with 20. That is 25 minutes before a 500-listing import becomes searchable, 100 minutes for a 2,000-video wave, 8.3 hours to re-embed 10k products. Fixed by dispatching `ceil(waiting / 20)` concurrent `pg_net` posts per tick, capped at 10 (**200 jobs/min**); idle behaviour unchanged, so an idle marketplace still spends zero invocations. **Proven: 200 jobs queued 20:16:52, queue at 0 by 20:17:17 — ONE tick, 25 seconds, versus 10 minutes before.** The independent usage view corroborates it: avg queue lag **467s -> 8.1s**.
  - **Concurrent workers cannot double-process, demonstrated rather than assumed** (this prompt's ground rule). With 30 messages queued, two successive `embedding_jobs_read(20, 90)` calls claimed 20 and 10 with **overlap: 0**. pgmq sets the visibility timeout in the same statement that returns the rows, and `VT_SECONDS = 90 > 60s` tick is what makes it hold across ticks too.
  - **A single oversized product description would have jammed the entire queue, permanently.** `products.search_text` concatenates `description`, and the table carries **no CHECK constraints at all** — verified. One >32k-char description 400s the whole OpenAI call, which carries the whole batch of 20, so one bad row fails 19 innocent jobs on every retry forever. Live max is 97 chars, so this was pure latent risk. Fixed at both ends: truncate to 30,000 chars (prevents), and dead-letter after `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5` deliveries using pgmq's `read_ct`, which has always been tracked and never read (contains). Added exponential backoff (90s -> 1h) on 429/5xx via a new `embedding_jobs_set_vt` RPC — a non-429 4xx deliberately does NOT back off, since waiting cannot fix a malformed request.
  - **Query-cache growth and embed-query cost abuse both closed.** `search_query_embeddings` had no eviction and kept every phrase ever typed (~3 KB each). New nightly `prune_search_query_embeddings()`: evicts unrepeated phrases (`hits < 5`) older than 30 days, then LRU down to a 200k hard cap — the cap is what makes growth *bounded* rather than merely slowed, since a scraper can mint novel phrases faster than 30 days retires them. Phrases with `hits >= 5` are never evicted by age; re-paying OpenAI to relearn a known repeat query is the one thing the cache exists to prevent. **This project had NO rate-limiting pattern anywhere** (checked migrations and src), so `embed-query` got the smallest viable one: fixed-window counters, applied ONLY on a cache MISS (a cached lookup costs nothing, so throttling it would punish heavy legitimate users and save no money). **Two scopes, and the global one is the point** — per-IP alone is defeated outright by IP rotation and would have left the actual bill unbounded. Both verified: 30 allowed / 31st refused / different IP unaffected; and with a global budget of 5, five distinct IPs pass and a **sixth call from a brand-new IP is refused.** Fails OPEN by design — a cost guard must not take search down with it.
  - **The health check now watches itself.** `embedding_pipeline_health()` worked and was called by nobody — the exact 3-day silent-failure mode it was built to catch still needed a human to think to run it. Two cron jobs, because pg_cron runs each job in one transaction and a job that RAISEs rolls back its own log row: `embedding-health-log` (*/10) records history and notifies admins **on transition into a bad state only** (a 3-day outage should produce one notification, not 432 — an alert channel that floods is one people mute), `embedding-health-alarm` (5-55/10) raises so the failure is visible in `cron.job_run_details`. Checked first: the project has `notifications` (per-profile, `profiles.is_admin`, 2 admins) and **no email/Slack/webhook integration anywhere**, so this is built on what exists rather than inventing a channel.
  - **`embedding_usage_daily` view** — jobs/day/source, chars, a deliberately crude est_usd (~4 chars/token, for spotting a 10x jump, explicitly NOT an invoice), and avg/max queue lag. Needed no new logging: pgmq's archive already retains `enqueued_at` and `archived_at`. It immediately paid for itself twice — corroborating the dispatch fix (467s -> 8.1s) and showing the 3-day outage still legible in the data as **42-hour average queue lag** on real product jobs.
  - **Added `products_live_category_idx`.** `products.category_id` had **no index at all** (only pkey, embedding, fts, trgm, status, vendor_id). `match_rfq_vendors` was building a per-vendor category array for EVERY vendor on the platform — `Seq Scan on products (rows=10026)` + an 854 kB sort, 15.6 ms of a 26 ms call — to answer one question. Rewritten as a direct category lookup, **proven equivalent on live data first** (same 10 vendors, zero rows in an EXCEPT in both directions): **16.9 ms -> 9.0 ms**.
  - **The video path is genuinely ready, and its own comment's claim was verified rather than trusted.** `match_videos` asserts in a comment that ordering by the bare `<=>` keeps the HNSW index usable and that `views_count`/`id` are tiebreakers only. At **2,501 videos** the plan confirms it: `Index Scan using product_videos_embedding_idx`, Incremental Sort handling the tiebreakers, **2.14 ms**. This is the one area the user explicitly flagged as growing, and it needed no change.
  - **Warm latencies at 10,033 products / 2,501 videos / 2,004 RFQs / 510 vendors:** `search_products` **6.8-18.4 ms** end-to-end on real cached OpenAI embeddings, `match_videos` 1.3 ms, `related_products` 3.1 ms, `for_you_products` 4.9 ms (200 rows), `match_rfq_vendors` 9.0 ms, `match_vendor_rfqs` 38.2 ms. **`zzzznotathing` still returns 0 rows at 10k products** — Master Prompt 5's `max_distance = 0.80`, calibrated on 26 products, still holds two orders of magnitude up.
  - **Two honest caveats on the numbers.** (1) Random 1536-dim vectors are the *pathological worst case* for HNSW — near-orthogonal, no cluster structure — so synthetic-vector timings overstate cost; measured avg distance 0.9989 with only 20 of 10,000 rows under the 0.80 threshold. The real-embedding `search_products` figures above are the trustworthy ones. (2) Every function shows a large first-call cost (~100-700 ms) that falls to single-digit ms on repeat with an identical plan and zero disk reads — process/cache warm-up, not I/O. At 10k concurrent users most queries are warm, but a cold path is real and is the number to watch.
  - **All synthetic data removed and the removal verified:** back to exactly 33 products / 1 video / 4 RFQs / 10 vendors, **0** synthetic rows in any table, 0 synthetic `auth.users`, 0 scaffold tables. Vendor `catalog_embedding`s recomputed from the real catalogue only (synthetic listings had been averaged into them), and real RFQ matching re-verified to reproduce the Part 1d numbers exactly.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` **0 errors**; eslint unchanged from baseline (3 pre-existing errors in `command.tsx`/`textarea.tsx`/`payments.ts`, none in touched files); `vite build` green; `scripts/search-smoke.mjs` **10/10**; ForYou browser check **4/4**; `get_advisors(security)` and `get_advisors(performance)` introduced nothing beyond two INFO "unused index" notes on indexes created minutes earlier; all 10 new functions/tables confirmed `anon: false, authenticated: false`; `embedding_pipeline_health()` reports **OK / pipeline healthy**.
- 2026-09-09 (Master Prompt 5): **First regression pass against REAL embeddings. Three genuine bugs found and fixed — one made every kids-wear search return zero results, another made every search return the entire catalogue.** New migrations `20260909120000_products_embedding_hnsw_index.sql`, `20260909130000_embedding_pipeline_health_and_loud_worker.sql`, `20260909140000_match_products_vector_distance_threshold.sql` (all **applied**); `src/lib/searchFilters.ts`, `scripts/search-smoke.mjs`, new `scripts/search-relevance-check.mjs`.
  - **Context: every prior "verification" of this system was against zero embeddings.** The Vault secret the worker needs did not exist for three days, so the pipeline had never fired once. It was added 2026-09-09 and the backfill drained in ~2 minutes. This is the first time any ranking, matching or personalisation logic has been measured against real vectors.
  - **The query cache had never been warmed — 0 rows.** Warmed it through the real browser flow (raw SQL cannot: `embed-query` is an edge function), 10 representative buyer phrases, one OpenAI call each, no waste. **Warm-then-retry verified 10/10:** a cold phrase makes two `search_products` calls (`embedding_used` false -> true) with an `embed-query` warm between them; the second page load makes ONE call reporting true. Never exercised end to end before.
  - **The relevance hand-check the original spec asked for and could never run.** `gym clothing` -> Mesh Training Tee, Mesh Panel Training Tee, Cotton Track Pants (all Activewear, zero keyword overlap; returned nothing at all before today). `wedding outfit` -> Chikankari Anarkali, Hand-Embroidered Kurta. `hand embroidered` -> Hand-Embroidered Kurta, Chikankari Anarkali (chikankari *is* hand embroidery). `summer beachwear` -> Linen Camp Shirt, Block-Print Sundress. `linen shirt` -> both linen shirts first. The semantic half works.
  - **BUG 1 (severe, user-visible): every kids/child/baby search returned ZERO results.** `searchFilters.ts` pre-scoped those queries to gender `"Boys"` — a value `products.gender` has never held. Live vocabulary is Men 11 / Women 10 / Unisex 4 / Kids 1. The facet also offered `Boys`/`Girls` (match nothing) and omitted `Kids` and `Unisex`, so the 4 unisex listings were unreachable through the facet AND silently dropped by any gender filter. Aligned the vocabulary to the column. Measured: `kids clothing`, `kids wear`, `baby`, `boys t-shirt`, `girls dress` all went **0 -> 1 result**. A code review would not have caught this — the code was internally consistent, it just disagreed with the database.
  - **BUG 2 (severe): with the vector half live, there was no such thing as a zero-result search.** Nearest-neighbour search always has a nearest, so `search_products('zzzznotathing')` returned **26 results — the entire live catalogue**, presented as matches. The `N results` count was meaningless and the honest empty state built in Master Prompt 1 had become unreachable. Fixed with a MEASURED cutoff: across all 26 live vectors, real queries' nearest neighbour sits at 0.305-0.515 (`linen shirt` 0.3049, `kurta` 0.4432, `gym clothing` 0.5152) while gibberish's nearest is **0.8301**. `max_distance = 0.80` sits in that gap. Result: gibberish 26 -> **0**, `kurta` 26 -> 25, others unchanged. The FTS branch is deliberately NOT thresholded — a literal keyword match is relevant however far apart the embeddings are. **Calibrated on 26 products; revisit as the catalogue grows.**
  - **I broke production search for ~2 minutes doing it, and the cause is worth recording.** `create or replace function` with an ADDED parameter does not replace anything — it creates a second overload. Both accepted the 3-argument call `search_products` makes, so Postgres raised `42725: function match_products(text, halfvec, integer) is not unique` and ALL search failed. Caught by testing rather than assuming; fixed by dropping the 4-arg version. The migration file now leads with that `drop` so a fresh deploy cannot recreate the ambiguity.
  - **BUG 3 (the incident itself): the cron reported success 3,960 times over a dead pipeline.** The worker's command was one statement guarded by `... where exists (select 1 from vault.decrypted_secrets ...)`. A SELECT whose WHERE is false returns zero rows and **succeeds**, so `cron.job_run_details.status` read `succeeded` on every run for three days while nothing was embedded. There was no error to find. The worker now RAISES when there is work it cannot do, so a missing secret becomes a visible `failed` within one minute. Added `embedding_pipeline_health()` (service_role only) for the other silent shape — a queue being read but never draining. No new tables, no new schedule.
  - **Added the missing `products_embedding_idx` HNSW index** that Master Prompt 1 deferred until after a backfill which then never ran. `CONCURRENTLY` worked through MCP `execute_sql` (no transaction wrapper); `apply_migration` DOES wrap, so the file notes the CLI path must drop the keyword and accept a brief write lock. Verified `indisvalid = true` after — a failed concurrent build leaves an INVALID index that is silently never used.
  - **Category-rename cascade proven end to end for the first time.** Renamed `Belts` -> `Belts & Buckles`: `category_name` re-synced immediately, `search_text` regenerated, a job was enqueued with the new text, the cron drained it, and the **embedding hash changed**. Renamed back; state fully restored. Previously only half-tested, because an earlier session's classifier blocked the taxonomy write.
  - **Phase 2's "pre-scoped exception" turned out to be already done.** `PostRequirement.tsx` no longer has the 17-tile hardcoded list — a prior session rewired it onto `sellerCategories.ts` through the same picker `Upload.tsx` uses, resolving `category_id` via `resolveSubcategoryId ?? resolveParentCategoryId`. The prompt's evidence (`category_match = false` on all 4 RFQs) was consistent with that fix being live: **all 4 RFQs date from July and predate it.** Proved it works rather than rebuilding it — inserted an open-pool RFQ carrying a real `category_id` and `match_vendor_rfqs` returned **`category_match: true`**, the first RFQ ever to do so. Test row deleted immediately; the orphaned queue job self-cleaned through the worker's deleted-row path, validating that branch too. Re-confirmed `enqueue_rfq_embedding` still excludes `category_id`, so a recategorisation costs no OpenAI call.
  - **RFQ->vendor matching is semantically sound on real data:** `Fabrics — Linen` -> **Mumbai Linen House** top (0.546); `Premium Cotton T-Shirts` -> **Tirupur Textiles** top (0.618, the knitwear hub that actually carries the cotton tees); the junk-titled `Quote request: Abhishek Mitra` correctly scores everything low (max 0.177).
  - **Phase 4 personalisation is correct and explainable per buyer:** Demo Buyer (0 prefs, 6 views) -> `taste`; Abhishek (3 prefs, 8 views) -> `taste`; Nevu Roby (1 pref, 1 view) -> `taste`; the four buyers with 0 prefs and 0 views -> `popularity`. `ForYou.tsx` surfaces `ranking.source` honestly and never infers personalisation client-side.
  - **Not fixed, deliberately, with reasons:** (a) `preferredVideoCategoryNames()` is stale as documented, but measured impact today is **zero** — the only live video is category `Buttons`, which no buyer preference maps to under the correct `pref_category_map` either; the fix converts a sync pure function into an async DB-backed one rippling through two components, which the phase's own guardrail says not to take on. (b) `ForYou.tsx` still renders **48 fabricated `PRODUCT_POOL` products** when the live catalogue is empty — the same "no mock data in production" violation removed from SearchResults in Master Prompt 1, but fixing it needs new loading/empty states, outside Phase 4's stated fix scope. (c) ~35 SECURITY DEFINER functions are `anon`-executable project-wide; every privileged one carries an internal `is_admin()` guard, so this is missing defence-in-depth rather than an exploitable hole — **but note `reject_vendor_content` is anon-executable again despite migration `20260801100327` having explicitly revoked it, so that hardening has regressed.** All three are candidates for their own prompt.
  - **Phase 5 verdict: no correctness or security bugs.** `engagement_events` has RLS + 2 policies, 119 real rows, and is live (called from `engagement.ts` / `vendorAnalytics.ts`). `log_engagement_event` is well built — `viewer_id` comes from `auth.uid()` inside the function so it cannot be forged, the vendor is derived server-side from the product/ad so traffic cannot be misattributed, and status guards mirror the counter RPCs. `vendor_store_unit_and_recommendations` contains **zero** embedding references and does not overlap the matching system.
  - **`get_advisors(security)` after all DDL: my changes introduced nothing.** `embedding_pipeline_health()` appears in neither the anon nor the authenticated list; the pre-existing baseline is unchanged.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` **0 errors** (the old 23-error baseline was cleared by intervening sessions — that note is now stale); eslint clean; `vite build` green; `scripts/search-smoke.mjs` **10/10** including the now-reachable empty state; `embedding_pipeline_health()` reports `OK / pipeline healthy` with the rescheduled worker running.
- 2026-09-09 (vendor backlog): **GST and CIN became real documents instead of typed numbers, the app stopped asking for an Aadhaar it never collected, KYC verdicts got a notification that goes somewhere, and admins can finally see a signed contract.** Migration: `handle_new_user_document_unreachable_conflict`. New: `src/components/VendorContractPanel.tsx` (Cosora-Admin). Changed: `src/pages/Onboarding.tsx`, `src/pages/NewArrivals.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/vendorOnboarding.ts`, `src/lib/queries/notifications.ts`, `src/lib/queries/signupProfile.ts`, `src/lib/notificationsStore.ts`, `tests/vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts`, `tests/new-arrivals.spec.ts`, `scripts/restore-demo-buyer-fixture.mjs`, and in Cosora-Admin `src/pages/VendorDetail.tsx` + `src/lib/database.types.ts`.
  - **Commit status, added 2026-09-11:** the Cosora-Admin files named in this entry (including `src/components/VendorContractPanel.tsx`, listed under New) were not committed when it was written. They reached GitHub on 2026-09-11 in Cosora-Admin `01ab71e` (`git ls-remote origin main` → `01ab71e5b549…`). See the Master Prompt 7 Phase 6 entry.
  - **DECISION — CIN is collected, Aadhaar is not, and the app stops asking for it.** Both had state, both flowed into `vendor_documents`, and neither had an input anywhere: `setCin`/`setAadhaar` had zero call sites while step 1's "documents required" dialog told every vendor to have both ready. CIN is now a real optional field with its own upload. **Aadhaar is deliberately NOT built**: retaining Aadhaar numbers or images is constrained by the Aadhaar Act 2016 / UIDAI rules for anyone who is not an authorised KUA/AUA, PAN already identifies the entity for B2B verification, and building the capture first and asking the compliance question later is the wrong order. The payload key, the column and the `doc_type` all stay so nothing that reads them breaks — only the collection is deferred, and the step-1 checklist no longer promises it. **CIN is optional and always will be until an entity-type field exists**: only companies and LLPs registered with the MCA have one, a proprietorship has none, and there is no field anywhere in the form to tell them apart (checked, not assumed).
  - **GST and CIN now carry the actual certificate.** `saveVendorOnboarding()` hardcoded `file_url: null` for both, so an admin was asked to approve or reject a number the vendor had typed with nothing to open. Both now upload through the same `uploadKycDocument()` as PAN — same private `business-docs` bucket, same `${vendorId}/kyc/…` path shape, same `assertKycBucket()` invariant. The three per-type upload handlers are one `makeKycUploadHandler` factory rather than three copies, so a fourth document type cannot quietly acquire a different bucket. **Verified end to end** on a real 9-step run: all three `vendor_documents` rows came back with a vendor-id-prefixed path, `verified = false`, and a working signed URL; `vendor_profiles.cin` was populated for the first time; and **no `aadhaar` row exists**, because nothing collects one.
  - **DECISION — a typed signature stays imageless, and the admin view says so.** `signature_url` is null whenever the vendor accepts the auto-generated cursive rendering instead of drawing. The alternative was to render that typed name to a canvas and store it — rejected: it would produce something that looks like a signature the vendor never made, which is the exact fabrication pattern this codebase has spent five passes removing. A typed signature is already complete evidence (`signed_name` + `agreement_version` + timestamp + the affirmative act), so the new admin panel renders **"typed — no image on file"** rather than a broken image or an unexplained blank.
  - **Admins can read the signed agreement.** New `VendorContractPanel` on Cosora-Admin's `/vendors/:id`: version, signed name, timestamp, and a 5-minute signed URL for the signature when there is one. **Read-only on purpose** — `vendor_contracts` has no UPDATE or DELETE policy for anyone, so a UI with an edit control would imply a capability the database does not grant. It also warns when a vendor holds more than one row and explains the two ways that happens (a legitimate re-sign at a NEW version, versus the historical duplicate at the same version from before `trg_vendor_contracts_one_per_version`). `vendor_contracts` had to be added to Cosora-Admin's `database.types.ts` — those types predate the table.
  - **KYC notifications now land somewhere.** `kyc_approved`/`kyc_rejected` were falling through `KIND_META` to the neutral fallback, and — because `href` was derived solely from `conversation_id`, which is null for them — linking to `/notifications`, the page the vendor is already on. Both kinds are now in the union and in `KIND_META` with a `/kyc` destination; `href` prefers a kind-specific link and only then falls back to the conversation rule. **This is wired end to end, not groundwork:** `set_vendor_document_verified()` writes both, and both rows exist in production data (observed: "Your PAN document was verified" / "…needs attention").
  - **`handle_new_user`'s `on conflict do nothing` is UNREACHABLE, and now says why.** `profiles_id_fkey` is `FOREIGN KEY (id) REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, so a profiles row cannot exist without its auth user, and deleting the auth user takes the profile with it — there is nothing that can be sitting there to collide with, and the seeded demo fixtures are not a counter-example for the same reason. (An earlier read of `information_schema` missed this FK because the referenced table is in the `auth` schema; `pg_constraint` is the reliable source.) The prompt's suggested fix — "update `active_role` when the existing value is null" — is doubly unreachable: the column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'buyer'`. The handler now does `on conflict do update` as **defence in depth** rather than a bug fix: it can only fill a null, and it only touches `active_role` when the signup metadata explicitly named a whitelisted role, so a row nobody's signup spoke for is untouched.
  - **`applyPendingSignupProfile` still swallows errors, but no longer silently.** The non-blocking behaviour is unchanged and deliberate — a failed write must never stop a sign-in. But it now runs on all three sign-in paths including the confirmation link most real users arrive by, so a persistent failure would lose every new vendor's brand name with nothing to show for it. The catch logs the user id and the role, because "it failed" is not actionable and "which account, which write path" is.
  - **One NEW suite failure, and it is not ours.** `video-closeups-bunny` T8.1 now fails in
    its `beforeAll` with "Bunny never finished encoding … (status=2)" — status 2 is
    "processing". Reproduced twice, so not a flake: Bunny is not finishing a short 478×850
    clip inside `ENCODE_TIMEOUT_MS = 6 min` (polled every 10s), which is generous. Nothing in
    this pass touches video upload, Bunny or moderation — check the Bunny library/account
    before suspecting the app, and if encoding is legitimately slower now, the timeout is the
    thing to raise. Because it fails in the fixture, T8.1b–T8.5 never run, so the whole Bunny
    suite is currently blocked behind it.
  - **A red test turned out to be the page's fault.** `new-arrivals.spec.ts` waited on `[role="tab"]` and timed out; the assumption was a stale selector. In fact it was written against `src/components/buyer/BuyerHomeTabs.tsx` — which has the right roles and labels and which **nothing imports**. The strip that renders lives inline in `NewArrivals.tsx` and was five plain links: no tablist, no `aria-selected`, and an active state **hardcoded to `/home/new-arrivals`** instead of derived from the route (correct only by accident, since that strip only renders on that page). The strip is now a real tablist with route-derived selection, and the spec asserts the original intent again — plus that exactly one tab is selected. `BuyerHomeTabs.tsx` is left in place as dead code rather than deleted, because another session is active in this repo.
- 2026-09-09: **Phase 3.8 — buyer geography. Ships the aggregation and the card; the map is deliberately NOT shipped, because its "no new dependency" premise was false for this repo.** New: `supabase/migrations/20260909060000_vendor_buyer_geography.sql` (applied), `src/lib/queries/buyerGeography.ts`, `scripts/vendor-buyer-geography-check.mjs`. Changed: `src/lib/queries/profile.ts`, `src/pages/AccountInfo.tsx`, `src/pages/Analytics.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`.
  - **Phase 0 — the prerequisite bug was real and worse than described.** `AccountInfo.tsx` asks every new buyer for a pincode and, on the "use my location" path, reverse-geocodes a **city and state** out of BigDataCloud as well — then used them for a toast message and dropped all three on the floor, because `saveAccountInfo()` never even accepted them as arguments. So `buyer_profiles.city/state/postal_code` was null for every account created through onboarding, which is why this feature had nothing to aggregate. `saveAccountInfo` now persists them, mirroring `saveProfileFull`'s column choice: `city` (the person's own city), never `business_city` — the geolocation reading is where the human is standing, not where their firm is registered, and conflating the two would corrupt a field the full profile form owns.
  - **The manual-pincode path deliberately stores less.** City/state are written **only** when geolocation actually resolved them; a typed pincode persists `postal_code` alone. There is no pincode gazetteer here, and deriving a city from a pincode would put a fabricated place on a vendor's map — the exact failure the rest of this analytics work exists to remove. Typing a pincode by hand also clears any previously detected city/state, so a stale place is never paired with a new pincode.
  - **No backfill, by instruction and on merit.** Every pre-existing buyer has no location and it cannot be recovered. The card states its own coverage instead ("Based on N of M recent visits with a known location") and that line renders whenever there is any traffic — a caveat that only appears when the news is bad trains the reader to ignore it when it does appear. Below 50% coverage it additionally says so in orange.
  - **`buyer_profiles` RLS is unchanged, and the check suite asserts it.** The vendor needs counts per city and must never gain read access to buyer rows — widening that policy even to "buyers who viewed my products" would hand every vendor a queryable list of their buyers' home cities joined to names and companies. `vendor_buyer_geography` is SECURITY DEFINER and does the join server-side, returning tallies only. A future change that "fixes" the card by loosening a policy fails `scripts/vendor-buyer-geography-check.mjs`.
  - **k-anonymity is enforced at both levels displayed, not just one.** Any city or state backed by fewer than 3 distinct `viewer_id`s folds into an unnamed `other` bucket that still carries the counts, so `named + other == events_with_location` (asserted). States are aggregated server-side rather than rolled up from the already-censored city list: a state with five viewers spread across three small cities is safe to name even though none of its cities is, and rolling up would discard that for no privacy gain.
  - **A real auth hole was found in my own guard and fixed before it shipped.** Copying `ad_category_benchmarks`' `if not (vid = auth.uid() or is_admin()) then return null` looked airtight and is not: with no JWT `auth.uid()` is NULL, so the whole expression is NULL, `if NULL then` does not fire, and the function **returns real data to an unauthenticated caller**. Caught by calling it as service_role during development — it answered with a full result. Now `coalesce(vid = auth.uid(), false) or coalesce(public.is_admin(), false)`, verified to return null with no JWT. **`ad_category_benchmarks` still has the original shape AND its default PUBLIC execute grant** — same hole, low impact (anonymised peer aggregates), flagged in `claude.md` rather than silently changed underneath `CompetitorAds.tsx`.
  - **A temp table was written and then removed before applying.** The first draft materialised the joined rows into `create temp table … on commit drop`. Wrong twice: a `STABLE` function must not perform DDL, and Supabase pools connections through PgBouncer, so a temp table can outlive its request and collide with a concurrent call on the same backend. Replaced with CTEs shared across the scalar subqueries — same single pass, none of the risk.
  - **`ad_impression` is excluded from the geography.** An impression is the platform choosing to render something, not a buyer expressing interest; counting it would let ad spend inflate a map meant to answer "where is demand". Only `product_view`, `profile_view` and `search_click` qualify. Asserted.
  - **THE MAP IS NOT BUILT, and the spec's premise for it was wrong.** "Reuse MapLibre GL JS — already the chosen library, so no new dependency" is true of **Cosora-Admin, a separate repo**. `maplibre-gl` is absent from this project's `package.json` and `node_modules`. Adding it would mean editing `package.json`/`package-lock.json` while a concurrent session is actively changing them, plus ~800 KB on a bundle already at 745 KB gzipped and a runtime tile-provider dependency on a page that otherwise needs nothing but Supabase. The only dependency-free alternative — hand-drawing Indian state boundaries as inline SVG — would mean **inventing geography**. So the card ships the ranked state/city list, the home-city marker and the coverage line; `states` is already shaped for a choropleth, so the map is an additive change to `BuyerGeographyCard`, not a rewrite.
  - **Four render states, kept distinct** (the rule this project already applies elsewhere): refused (not your vendor), no visits at all, visits but zero with a known location, and real data. The third is the one that would otherwise read as "feature broken".
  - **Verified:** `node scripts/vendor-buyer-geography-check.mjs` — **19/19**, writes real rows and restores everything including the buyer's original city. Covers the Phase 0 columns being writable, `buyer_profiles` RLS unchanged in both directions, coverage counting, suppression below the threshold, **admission above it** (three distinct viewers in one city IS named — without this, a function returning nothing at all would have passed every other check), totals reconciling, `ad_impression` exclusion, the buyer/anon/admin guard matrix, and `home_location` matching `vendor_profiles`. Plus a browser pass over both the empty and populated card. `vite build` clean; zero type errors in files this work touched.
  - **One test bug worth recording**, because its failure message was actively misleading: the `ad_impression` check compared against a baseline captured before a later step inserted three more events, so it reported `3 -> 6` and blamed the ad impression for rows the script itself had created. The baseline is now re-read immediately before the assertion.
- 2026-09-09 (contract integrity): **A vendor could destroy their own signed supplier agreement from the browser. Closed, along with the duplicate-contract and KYC-storage-leak bugs that surfaced alongside it — and the two carryover fixes are now proved on the paths that actually matter.** Migrations: `protect_vendor_contracts_from_cascade_delete`, `vendor_contracts_one_signature_per_agreement_version`. New: `scripts/purge-orphaned-kyc-objects.mjs`, `scripts/vendor-contract-integrity-check.mjs`, `tests/mp5-phase4-confirmation-link.spec.ts`. Changed: `src/lib/queries/vendorOnboarding.ts`, `tests/vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts`, `tests/mp4-phase2-callback-onboarding.spec.ts`.
  - **P0: `vendor_contracts` was not append-only, and a foreign key was the way around it.** The table has SELECT and INSERT policies and no UPDATE or DELETE for anyone, admins included — that is the whole basis of "an editable contract is not evidence". But `vendor_contracts.vendor_id` referenced `vendor_profiles(id)` **ON DELETE CASCADE**, and `vprofiles_write` was one `FOR ALL` policy whose USING clause included `id = auth.uid()` — and ALL includes DELETE. So `delete from vendor_profiles where id = auth.uid()` from a vendor's own anon-key session took every signed contract with it. **Demonstrated, not theorised:** deleting demo-buyer's profile row through an RLS-enforced anon client took its `vendor_contracts` count from 1 to 0. Fixed at both layers, because either alone is insufficient — RESTRICT still leaves the vendor able to delete their profile, and an admin-only DELETE policy still leaves an admin able to erase contracts by cascade: the FK is now **ON DELETE RESTRICT** (`confdeltype` `c` → `r`), and `vprofiles_write` is split into `vprofiles_insert` / `vprofiles_update` / `vprofiles_delete`, the last gated to `is_admin() and admin_role() in ('super_admin','vendor_ops')`. **Nothing in the product deletes a vendor_profiles row** — grepped before removing it; the only callers were a test spec and its restore script — so no feature was taken away. The suspend/reinstate path (`set_account_status()`) is the precedent for "this vendor should stop existing"; a profile gets deactivated, not deleted, for the same reason an order is not un-created. **6/6 assertions green**, and every one reads the row back rather than trusting the return value, because a DELETE that RLS denies matches zero rows and *returns success*.
  - **P0: a retried onboarding submit signed a second permanent contract.** `saveVendorOnboarding()` delete-then-inserts `vendor_documents` for exactly this reason and had no equivalent guard on contracts; with no UPDATE or DELETE policy, the duplicate could never be removed by anything. Now one signature per `(vendor_id, agreement_version)`, enforced by `trg_vendor_contracts_one_per_version` plus a client-side check-then-insert. **It is a trigger and not a UNIQUE constraint on purpose:** vendor `9ddda61f-…` already holds two rows at `2026-09-v1`, deliberately preserved as the record of the bug, and a unique constraint cannot be created over existing duplicates — Postgres has no NOT VALID for unique constraints, only CHECK and FOREIGN KEY. The alternatives were deleting the evidence or hardcoding that vendor's UUID into a partial index forever. The trigger **skips** rather than raises, because a retried submit has already persisted the profile, documents and product and the contract for that exact version demonstrably exists — that is idempotency, not a hidden denial; the silent-success trap this repo guards against is about *authorization* being refused invisibly. A re-sign after `SUPPLIER_AGREEMENT_VERSION` changes is a different version and still inserts. **Proved end to end:** demo-buyer driven through the real 9-step form **twice** now holds exactly **1** contract row.
  - **P0: every KYC re-submission stranded the superseded scan in the private bucket.** Storage is not covered by a row delete and there is no cascade, so the old identity document stayed in `business-docs` referenced by nothing and, after the fact, indistinguishable from a real vendor's. `saveVendorOnboarding()` now reads the `file_url`s of the rows it is about to replace *before* deleting them and removes those objects *after* the row delete succeeds — that ORDER is deliberate: a failed storage delete then leaves a harmless orphan rather than a live row pointing at a file that is gone. Legacy full-URL values from before the public→private move are skipped rather than guessed at, and the whole step is best-effort so a registration never fails over tidying. **12 existing orphans cleared, 12 → 0**, via `scripts/purge-orphaned-kyc-objects.mjs`, which *computes* the orphan set (an object is a candidate only if no `vendor_documents.file_url` and no `vendor_contracts.signature_url` names it — so a signature PNG is never a candidate), deletes as each object's **owner under RLS** rather than with a service-role key, and is dry-run by default. Two further full onboarding runs afterwards left the count at **0**.
  - **Why a BUYER account had KYC uploads.** `uploadKycDocument()` has exactly one call site, `Onboarding.tsx:224`, and **`/onboarding` has no role gate at all** — no guard in `App.tsx`, no role check in the page. That is by design: "Buyer → Vendor requires completing full vendor onboarding first", so a buyer walking into `/onboarding` is the intended way to become a vendor, and finishing it sets `active_role = 'seller'`. The demo-buyer objects specifically were residue from `vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts`, which drives that account through the real form on purpose. **The residual gap:** a buyer who *abandons* onboarding after uploading a PAN leaves KYC objects under an identity with no `vendor_profiles` row and no vendor-side `/kyc` page to manage them.
  - **The two carryover fixes were real but uncommitted, which is why a fresh clone still showed them broken.** `AuthCallback.tsx` calling `applyPendingSignupProfile()` and the deletion of `computeProfileScore()` both existed only in the working tree at `2279630`. The corroborating artifact: the throwaway vendor's stored `profile_score` is **29**, and the deleted nine-check formula was `filled/9 × 100` over checks that vendor satisfied completely — it would have written **100**. 29 is exactly `calculateProfileScore`'s weighted sum for that row (contactDetails 8 + category 7 + email 7 + website 7).
  - **AuthCallback is now proved on the confirmation-link path itself, not by a session injection.** `src/lib/supabase.ts` sets `detectSessionInUrl: true` on the implicit flow, so Supabase's `/auth/v1/verify?type=signup` redirects to `/auth/callback#access_token=…&refresh_token=…`. `tests/mp5-phase4-confirmation-link.spec.ts` navigates a **completely cold browser context** — asserted empty `localStorage`, no injected session, Login.tsx never loaded — to exactly that URL shape. New account `zz-mp5-link@cosora.in`: before, `raw_user_meta_data.brand_name = "Kesar Textiles"` with **zero** `vendor_profiles` rows; after, `vendor_profiles.brand_name = "Kesar Textiles"`. The one thing not reproduced is the verify endpoint minting the tokens (no mailbox for the throwaway domain, no service-role key here) — nothing under test depends on their provenance, only on a session arriving *in the URL*.
  - **Profile score re-verified on a second, genuinely fresh vendor** (`28ada0e2-…`, the Phase 4 account): stored at submit **29** · displayed on `/business-profile-score` **29** · after the dashboard's recompute **29**.
  - **The test harness had to change with the database, and the old teardown would have passed while doing nothing.** `vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts` deleted `vendor_profiles` in cleanup — now impossible for a client and impossible for anyone once a contract references the row. Since an RLS-denied DELETE matches zero rows and returns success, leaving it would have "passed" forever while cleaning nothing. The row is now **blanked** rather than deleted, and the `beforeAll` guard changed from "no `vendor_profiles` row" to "not `onboarding_complete`", which is equivalent for everything the spec asserts.
- 2026-09-09 (prove the write path): **The signup metadata loss is fixed, and for the first time a vendor has actually been through registration end to end — `vendor_contracts` went from 0 rows to a real signed contract, and every `vendor_profiles` completeness count moved off a baseline that two previous passes left untouched.** Also: one profile-score formula instead of two, and four things nobody asked about, three of which are gaps rather than wins. New: `tests/mp4-phase1-register.spec.ts`, `tests/mp4-phase2-callback-onboarding.spec.ts`, `tests/mp4-phase5-kyc-review.spec.ts`, `scripts/restore-demo-buyer-fixture.mjs`. Changed: `src/pages/AuthCallback.tsx`, `src/pages/Onboarding.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDashboard.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorOnboarding.ts`, `tests/vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts`.
  - **P0: every user who confirmed their email lost the name they typed at signup.** `handle_new_user()` writes exactly email, full_name, phone and active_role — read from `pg_get_functiondef`, not assumed. The brand name (seller) / company (buyer) rides in `raw_user_meta_data` until `applyPendingSignupProfile()` moves it, and that write needs a session, which a confirmation-gated signup does not have. `AuthCallback.tsx` is the first moment one exists — and it never called the function. `grep` showed exactly two call sites, Login.tsx:141 and Register.tsx:106, and the file had not been touched since its first commit. **Proved before and after at the row level, not the redirect level:** a real signup through `/register` left `raw_user_meta_data.brand_name = "Meridian Weaves"` with **zero** `vendor_profiles` rows; after the fix, hitting `/auth/callback` with a session injected directly (never through `/login`, whose own call would have proved the wrong site) produced `vendor_profiles.brand_name = "Meridian Weaves"`. Awaited inside the effect so a slow write cannot lose the race with the redirect that unmounts the page.
  - **A vendor went through all nine onboarding steps for real, and the rows are the evidence.** `zz-mp4-vendor@cosora.in` / `9ddda61f-d778-41a5-b568-39fd9f3eb37a`, created through the actual form, confirmed out of band (no service-role key in this environment, so the direct-SQL equivalent of `admin.updateUserById({email_confirm:true})`), then driven through business details → address → owner → category → premises photos → PAN upload → GSTIN → product → **drawn** signature. Landed: `brand_name`, `pan=AFZPK7190K`, `gstin=24AFZPK7190K1ZT`, `state=Gujarat`, `postal_code=394221`, 2 office photos, `onboarding_complete=true`, one `under_review` product with its image, a `vendor_documents` PAN row whose `file_url` is a **private storage path** (not a URL) resolving only through a minted signed URL, and a `vendor_contracts` row at `agreement_version = 2026-09-v1` with an uploaded signature PNG. Counts moved `7/1/0/0/0/0` → `8/2/1/1/1/1`, `vendor_contracts` 0 → 1. **The vendor is left in place deliberately.**
  - **Two profile-score formulas became one.** `computeProfileScore()` in vendorOnboarding.ts (9 unweighted booleans over the in-memory payload) is deleted. `calculateProfileScore()` (15 weighted signals over the rows) is now the only formula; `profileScoreInputFrom()` is the only snake_case→camelCase mapping and `PROFILE_SCORE_COLUMNS` the only select list, both shared with `fetchVendorDashboard`; `syncProfileScore(vendorId)` is the only writer outside the dashboard. It runs **last** in `saveVendorOnboarding` because it scores rows, not intentions — the step-8 product has to exist first — and is non-blocking, matching the two adjacent precedents, because failing a submit that already persisted everything real over a derived integer is the worse outcome. Verified on the throwaway vendor: **stored at submit = 29, displayed on /business-profile-score = 29, after the dashboard's own recompute = 29.** The recompute is now a no-op instead of a silent correction.
  - **NOT ANTICIPATED — the append-only contract is not append-only.** `vendor_contracts` has select and insert policies and no update or delete for anyone, which is what "an editable contract is not evidence" rests on. But `vendor_contracts.vendor_id` references `vendor_profiles(id)` **ON DELETE CASCADE**, and `vprofiles_write` is a single `ALL` policy with `id = auth.uid()` — so **a vendor can delete their own vendor_profiles row from the browser with the anon key and their signed agreement goes with it.** Demonstrated accidentally and then confirmed: deleting demo-buyer's profile row through an RLS-enforced anon client took its contract from 1 to 0. Same shape as the `vendor_documents` self-verify hole — an integrity guarantee defeated by a path nobody checked. **Left unfixed and flagged**: the fix is a product decision (should a vendor be able to delete their own profile at all?) and the test harness currently depends on that delete working.
  - **NOT ANTICIPATED — a retried onboarding submit signs a second permanent contract.** `saveVendorOnboarding` delete-then-inserts `vendor_documents` for idempotency but has no equivalent for `vendor_contracts`, so re-running onboarding inserted a second row for the same vendor at the same agreement version — and nothing, admin included, can remove it. Observed live when the full suite re-ran the Phase 2 spec. The spec now skips when the vendor is already onboarded; **the product gap stands.**
  - **NOT ANTICIPATED — a superseded KYC scan is leaked forever.** Re-submitting KYC rewrites the `vendor_documents` row but never removes the storage object the old row pointed at, so the previous identity scan stays in the private bucket referenced by nothing. Watched happen: `…/kyc/1788896389862-6ol41h.png` was referenced, then became an orphan when a re-run replaced it. This is also the mechanism behind the three "mystery" orphans Phase 3 went looking for.
  - **`cin` and `aadhaar` are wired end to end and have no input.** Both are in `VendorOnboardingPayload`, both create a `vendor_documents` row — and `setCin`/`setAadhaar` have **no call site anywhere in Onboarding.tsx**. `vendor_profiles.cin` can only ever be null, those documents can never exist, and step 1's "documents required for registration" dialog still tells vendors to have their Aadhaar and CIN ready. Only PAN and GST are reachable.
  - **Phase 3 stopped instead of deleting, as instructed — and the UUID was not a mystery.** The three orphaned objects under `11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111` are **demo-buyer@cosora.dev**, a live account: absent from `vendor_profiles` (its own test teardown removes that row) but present in `profiles` and `auth.users`. They are residue from `vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts`, which uploaded a PAN scan on every run, deleted the row and left the file. That spec now reads the storage paths off the rows **before** deleting them and removes exactly those — never a prefix listing, so it cannot reach objects it did not create. The three pre-existing objects are left for a human call.
  - **The write-path spec had been failing since the private-bucket change, and nobody had run it.** It asserted `fetch(pan.file_url)` — correct while KYC lived in the public bucket, and `TypeError: Failed to parse URL` ever since `file_url` became a storage path. Now mints a signed URL, which is the only read path a private object has.
  - **A `<canvas>` inside a vaul `Drawer` needs `data-vaul-no-drag`** (added to the signature canvas). vaul reads a pointer drag across drawer content as swipe-to-dismiss; `touch-action: none` covers touch only, browsers ignore it for a mouse, and `onOpenChange` nulls the signature when the drawer closes unsaved — so a dismissed drawer discards the drawing silently. Added defensively after reading vaul's `shouldDrag`; **honesty note:** the test failure that led here had a different cause (a bounding box read while the drawer was still animating, so the clicks landed on the overlay), so the guard is reasoned-from-source, not observed-in-the-wild.
  - **Supabase's built-in SMTP rate limit is a production blocker, reproduced again.** `tests/vendor-signup.spec.ts` fails with no user row created and no code defect behind it. A burst of genuine signups will be rejected until custom SMTP is configured in Supabase Auth — nothing in this repo can fix it.
  - **Phase 5: the Cosora-Admin KYC panel exists** (`src/components/VendorKycPanel.tsx`, mounted in `VendorDetail.tsx:169`, calling `set_vendor_document_verified` and gated on `canWrite(role,"accounts")`). The verdict round-trip was exercised against the throwaway vendor's real PAN row: a **non-admin was refused** and the row was untouched; a **reasonless rejection was refused**; a real rejection stored the reason and stamped `reviewed_by`; the vendor's `/kyc` page rendered "Rejected" with the reason and opened the scan through a freshly minted signed URL; **approve** flipped `verified` and cleared the reason; an unknown doc id raised rather than no-oping; both `kyc_approved` and `kyc_rejected` notifications fired; and `vendor_profiles.is_verified` stayed **false** — KYC approval still does not grant the trust seal. **What was NOT exercised: the admin panel's own buttons**, because no admin credentials are available in this environment and probing for one was correctly blocked. The panel's write path is read-and-reasoned, not clicked.
