Changed: `src/lib/queries/vendorOnboarding.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDocuments.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorStore.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDashboard.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendor.ts`, `src/lib/queries/profile.ts`, `src/lib/profileStore.ts`, `src/lib/quotesData.ts`, `src/lib/i18n.ts`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`, `src/pages/Kyc.tsx`, `src/pages/Register.tsx`, `src/pages/Login.tsx`, `src/pages/RoleSelection.tsx`, `src/pages/Onboarding.tsx`, `src/pages/BusinessProfile.tsx`, `src/pages/BusinessProfileScorePage.tsx`, `src/pages/BusinessTools.tsx`, `src/pages/MyStore.tsx`, `src/pages/VendorProfile.tsx`, `src/App.tsx`, `package.json`, and in Cosora-Admin `src/pages/VendorDetail.tsx`. Deleted: `src/pages/OtpVerify.tsx`, `src/pages/AppFeedback.tsx`.
  - **Commit status, added 2026-09-11:** the Cosora-Admin files named in this entry (including `src/components/VendorKycPanel.tsx`, listed under New) were not committed when it was written. They reached GitHub on 2026-09-11 in Cosora-Admin `01ab71e` (`git ls-remote origin main` → `01ab71e5b549…`). See the Master Prompt 7 Phase 6 entry.
  - **P0: KYC documents were fetchable by anyone with the URL, and that was measured, not assumed.** `uploadKycDocument()` wrote PAN scans to the PUBLIC `product-images` bucket and stored `getPublicUrl()` in `vendor_documents.file_url`. An unauthenticated `GET` of one of those URLs returned **HTTP 200, 70 bytes** — recorded before the fix. Now: the private `business-docs` bucket, the function returns a storage PATH (never a URL), and reads are 5-minute signed URLs minted **on demand for the one document being opened**, because a signed URL is a bearer token and a page view should not hand out credentials for documents nobody clicked. `getPublicUrl()` on a private bucket returns a string that 400s — worse than an error, because it looks like it worked — which is why the return type changed rather than the bucket name alone.
  - **The `${vendorId}/kyc/…` path shape is load-bearing and is now documented as such.** `business_docs_owner_select` is `foldername(name)[1] = auth.uid() OR is_admin()`, so flattening the path would make every document either unreadable or readable by the wrong vendor. `assertKycBucket()` runs at the top of every function that touches a `/kyc/` path and throws otherwise — a one-line invariant instead of a comment nobody reads.
  - **Three already-exposed objects were moved and purged.** `scripts/migrate-kyc-to-private-bucket.mjs` does copy → verify the copy resolves → repoint any referencing row → delete the public original, then re-lists to prove the negative. All three were under `11111111-…/kyc/` and were **orphans** — the previous pass's cleanup deleted the `vendor_documents` rows but left the storage objects, so nothing referenced them. 7/7 checks green; the public bucket now returns zero objects matching `%/kyc/%`. Verified afterwards: the old public URL **400s**, a signed URL works for the owner (200), the same URL **400s after its TTL**, a DIFFERENT vendor cannot sign the path at all ("Object not found" — the policy hiding it), and anon cannot sign it.
  - **A vendor could mark their own KYC documents verified. This is the thing this pass found that nothing had asked about.** `vendor_documents_all` is a single `ALL` policy with `(vendor_id = auth.uid()) OR is_admin()` on both USING and WITH CHECK, so `update vendor_documents set verified = true where id = <own row>` succeeded from the browser — proved against the live project before writing the fix. The anon key ships in the bundle, so this was a one-line self-service trust badge that defeated the entire point of KYC. The policy is deliberately **left in place** (a vendor must still read, insert and delete their own documents); a `before insert or update` trigger now refuses the four review columns to non-admins and forces `verified = false` on insert.
  - **`set_vendor_document_verified()` is the only writer, authorized inside the function.** SECURITY DEFINER, gated to `is_admin() and admin_role() in ('support','super_admin')` — the same predicate `set_account_status()` uses — because a client UPDATE that RLS denies matches zero rows and **returns success**, a trap this repo has already been bitten by. It refuses a rejection with no reason, clears the reason on approve (mirroring `approve_vendor_content`), raises on an unknown id rather than silently no-oping, and notifies the vendor. Eight assertions green, including "a non-admin calling the RPC is refused" and "an unknown doc id raises".
  - **The admin panel can now see and rule on a KYC document.** New `VendorKycPanel` on Cosora-Admin's `/vendors/:id`: the documents, their status, a signed "View document" action and per-document approve/reject with a required reason. Its write gate is `canWrite(role, "accounts")`, **not** `"vendors"` — the DB gate is support/super_admin and matching the button to the function keeps a disabled control from being the only thing between a wrong role and a 42501. No new storage policy was needed: `is_admin()` is already the second branch of `business_docs_owner_select`, verified before any UI was written.
  - **Approving KYC deliberately does NOT grant the trust seal.** The seal has three sources and this is not one of them; auto-granting would make a silent fourth and stop the verification card describing what buyers actually see. KYC status appears as a row IN that card, labelled "does not grant a seal", because "have we seen their PAN?" is the question a human asks before pressing the button.
  - **`Register.tsx` made zero Supabase calls, and picking "Seller" skipped the details step entirely** — so a seller supplied no name, no email and no password, and no account was created at all. Both roles now complete both steps and the form calls `auth.signUp()` for real, with `full_name`/`phone`/`active_role`/`brand_name` in metadata. Duplicate-email, weak-password and rate-limit errors are surfaced (the rate-limit branch was observed live during verification, which is how we know it works).
  - **Email confirmation is ON, so a signup has no session — and the UI now says so.** `auth.signUp()` returns a user and `session: null`; the form therefore ends on a "check your email" screen instead of routing to a dashboard the account cannot load. **`handle_new_user` was extended to read `active_role` from `raw_user_meta_data`**, because with no session the client cannot write it afterwards and a seller confirmed as a buyer is a bug you notice much later. That metadata is client-supplied, so the role is whitelisted to `buyer`/`seller` there — `is_admin` is deliberately not settable from it. Proved with a real signup: `profiles` came back with `full_name`, `phone` and **`active_role = 'seller'`**.
  - **Phone OTP: not configured, so every affordance that pretended otherwise is gone.** `supabase.auth.signInWithOtp({ phone })` returns `phone_provider_disabled / "Unsupported phone provider"` on this project — that one probe decided three separate items. `Login.handleSendCode`'s hardcoded `REGISTERED_PHONES` set and its `setTimeout(400)` are deleted and replaced with real email+password sign-in; the phone control is a labelled "coming soon" row (the country picker went with it, being unreachable UI). **`/auth/otp-verify` and `OtpVerify.tsx` are deleted** — the page accepted ANY six digits and then hardcoded `setRole("buyer")`, silently converting a vendor into a buyer. Onboarding step 2's "Verify" button, its OTP dialog and its green "Mobile verified" tick are gone; the number is a plain contact field.
  - **`RoleSelection`'s `if (session)` guard was not dead, it was silent.** Every route that navigates there now has a session, but a signed-out user can still type the URL — and the old code skipped the write and carried on to `/seller`, so the choice was recorded nowhere. It now sends them to sign in.
  - **The six deferred decisions, decided.** `vendor_contracts` **built** (append-only: select + insert policies and no update or delete for anyone, admins included — an editable contract is not evidence), written inside `saveVendorOnboarding()` in the same call that sets `onboarding_complete`, so "onboarded with no contract on file" is unreachable; the signature PNG goes to `business-docs` under `${vendorId}/contract/`. `agreement_version` comes from one constant that is both rendered and stored. App feedback **relabelled** to "Contact support" → `/help` and the page deleted. Offers and Q&A tiles **removed** (Q&A flagged as the higher-value deferral). `annual_turnover` **added** with a range picker; `capacity` **added** as `text[]`, persisted, and — the part that makes it worth having — **exposed to buyers on `/vendor/:id`**, where it replaced a module-scope `["Medium", "Export-grade"]` that every vendor on the platform was advertising.
  - **Profile score reweighted, stated numerically because a score that changes meaning silently stops being trusted.** Two signals added at 4 points each; the eight points came from the six heaviest existing signals rather than shaving all of them, so nothing moved by more than two: contactDetails 9→8, aboutUs 9→8, officePhotos 8→7, email 8→7, tenProducts 12→10, reviews 11→9. Total still exactly 100. The checklist page grew from 13 tiles to 15 and the full-width "employees" tile became a normal one — it existed only to make 12+1 divide evenly.
  - **`useState("Delhi NCR")` and a map iframe hardcoded to `q=Delhi%20NCR`** sat in the address step that had just been rewritten. A Surat mill was shown a map of Delhi. City defaults to empty and the map follows the typed pincode/city, rendering a prompt instead of a map when there is nothing to point at.
  - **Typecheck: 23 → 0, and two of the fixes were real bugs hiding behind type errors.** Five root causes. (1) `i18n.ts` had **five duplicate keys** within the Hindi dictionary — identical values, so no behaviour change, but edit one and the other silently wins. (2) `vendorDashboard.count()` took `table: string`, so `supabase.from(table)` had to describe every table at once and the filter columns intersected to `never` — **eleven errors, half the baseline, from one loose parameter**; narrowing it to a union of the four real tables fixed all of them. (3) Four `.upsert()` calls passed `Record<string, unknown>` or a computed key; typed against the table's `Insert` type instead. (4) Five settings/`SizeQty` **interfaces** written into `jsonb` columns — an interface has no implicit index signature so it is not assignable to `Json`; converted to `type` aliases, which is the actual rule rather than a cast. (5) `VendorProfile` passed `reviews: string` (a formatted *enquiry* count) into `SavedProduct.reviews: number` — and once that stopped masking it, a **second** bug appeared: a local capitalised `Gender` ("Unisex") being asserted into the store's lowercase union. Both fixed at the boundary; the local type is now `GenderFilter` so the collision cannot return. `npm run typecheck` was added so the wrong invocation (`tsc --noEmit` with no `-p`, which compiles nothing and reports a false 0) cannot be repeated.
  - **Still open, and stated rather than buried:** the supplier agreement TEXT has not been legally reviewed — storing the record is the engineering task, approving the words is not, and it needs Mitra's sign-off before real vendors are held to it. Phase 4's end-to-end onboarding run is gated on confirming a test account by hand, because email confirmation is on and the throwaway address has no mailbox.
- 2026-09-08 (my-store pass): **The vendor "My Store" cluster stops fabricating, and `/onboarding` starts saving. Every demo array, placeholder literal and dead control across `/my-store`, `/my-store/business`, `/my-store/business/tools`, `/business-profile`, `/business-profile-score` replaced with the vendor's own rows — plus the registration form that was silently dropping eight of the fields it collected.** New: `src/pages/Kyc.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDocuments.ts`, `src/components/vendor/AddBusinessCategoriesModal.tsx`, `src/components/vendor/ReviewLinkShare.tsx`, `src/data/businessCategoryGroups.ts`, `supabase/migrations/20260907180000_vendor_store_unit_and_recommendations.sql`, `tests/vendor-my-store.spec.ts`, `tests/vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts`. Changed: `src/pages/Onboarding.tsx`, `src/pages/MyStore.tsx`, `src/pages/MyBusiness.tsx`, `src/pages/BusinessTools.tsx`, `src/pages/BusinessProfile.tsx`, `src/pages/BusinessProfileScorePage.tsx`, `src/pages/Reviews.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/vendorOnboarding.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorStore.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDashboard.ts`, `src/lib/queries/products.ts`, `src/lib/queries/videos.ts`, `src/components/layout/DashboardSidebar.tsx`, `src/hooks/useVendorData.ts`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`. Deleted: `src/pages/BusinessProfileEmployees.tsx`.
  - **The registration form was the root cause, so it was fixed first.** Live count before this work: **7 vendor rows, 1 onboarded, 0 with pan, 0 with gstin, 0 with state, 0 with postal_code, 0 with office_photos.** Nothing downstream could show real data because nothing upstream was writing it. `submitContract()` wrapped the whole save in `if (user)` and then showed the success screen **unconditionally** — a signed-out vendor completed eight steps, saw "Welcome to Cosora", and had written nothing anywhere. A save that *threw* did the same. Both now block on step 9 with a visible error and the form intact; a missing session additionally links to sign-in. Because premises photos and the PAN scan now upload under the vendor's own id, a signed-out registration is in practice refused at **step 6** — earlier than the guard, which is the better outcome.
  - **Three uploads were `URL.createObjectURL()` blobs — alive only in the tab that made them.** Step 5's business images, step 6's PAN document and step 7's product images all handed `blob:` URLs to the database. They rendered convincingly for the vendor who picked them and were dangling pointers to everyone else, including the vendor after a reload. All three now upload to storage first and persist the public URL (`uploadVendorGalleryImage`, plus new `uploadKycDocument` and `uploadOnboardingProductImage`). `vendor_documents.file_url` was **always null** before this; the KYC upload was required to advance and then discarded.
  - **`state` and `postalCode` were already in the payload type, already written by `saveVendorOnboarding`, and already joined into `fullAddress` on `/business-profile` — the form simply never asked for them.** Added to step 3 (pincode: 6 digits, numeric, required), along with `landmark`, which was collected and saved all along but omitted from the address join, so a vendor who typed "opposite the bus depot" never saw it again anywhere in the app.
  - **A Business Category step was added, because a vendor with an empty `category` is invisible to category search.** The column was only reachable from `/business-profile`, after registration, if the vendor happened to find it. The picker was extracted out of `BusinessProfile.tsx` into `src/components/vendor/AddBusinessCategoriesModal.tsx` (+ `src/data/businessCategoryGroups.ts`) and is now the **same component** in both places, not a copy. Onboarding is 9 steps as a result; the three success-interstitial flags were renamed off their step numbers (`ownerSuccess`/`documentsSuccess`/`productSuccess`) so the coupling cannot rot again.
  - **The fake verification theatre is gone.** The OTP modal auto-filled `482931` after 900ms. `verifyPanName()` **failed when the name contained "fearce"** — and the field was pre-seeded with "Fearce Textiles Pvt Ltd", so the form's default state was a failing verification: debug logic shipped as product. Both replaced with a real format check that reports "**Submitted for review**", plus a line saying a human verifies it within 24–48h. `vendor_documents.verified` is flipped by an admin and by nothing in this app, so a green "Verified" tick derived from a regex and a 1.2-second timer was the app vouching for a document nobody had looked at. Also removed: the seeded name "Anandita", and `Vendor ID: 21935326` — a constant, identical for every vendor, and not an id this system issues.
  - **`unit`, `sizes` and `colours` were collected on step 7 and dropped on the floor.** The master prompt said the columns existed; **`products.unit` did not exist at all**, and `colour` is singular `text`, not an array. Added `unit` by migration; `sizes` (already `text[]`) is written; `colour` follows the repo's existing single-value convention (`resolveColour()` in `Upload.tsx`) and the form now **says so on screen** when more than one is picked rather than silently storing one of four. `fetchMyProducts` reads the real unit instead of hardcoding `"Piece"` for every listing in the catalogue.
  - **`uploadVendorImage()` had existed in `vendorStore.ts` with ZERO callers anywhere in the repo — which is exactly why `logo_url` was null for every vendor in the database.** `/my-store` is its first caller (logo) and `/business-profile` its second (banner). The banner case was the worse one: the column exists, the uploader existed, and the **buyer-facing** `/vendor/:id` has always rendered it — so buyers could see a banner the vendor had no way to upload, while the vendor's own page painted a static `DEMO_BANNER`.
  - **`/my-store` imported no query hooks at all.** Every vendor saw the same header: a `picsum.photos` stock portrait, the words "business name", somebody's phone number, `0.0` ratings and `0` followers. Now wired to `useAuth` + `useMyVendorProfile` + `useProfileScoreState`, with skeletons while loading, initials on a `#256fef` tint when there is no logo, and an empty state pointing at `/onboarding` when there is no vendor row.
  - **Logging out did not log you out.** Both `MyStore`'s confirm dialog and `DashboardSidebar.handleSignOut` called `navigate("/")` only — the session survived, so pressing Back left you signed in. Both now `await signOut()` first.
  - **The TrustedSEAL rendered unconditionally, so every vendor saw themselves as verified** — including the ones buyers see no seal for. Now gated on `trustSealFromParts()`, the **same** helper `/vendor/:id` and every product card use (admin verification, an active paid plan, or an ad-bought window), so the vendor sees exactly the badge buyers see. Unverified vendors get a "Get verified" chip → `/advertisements`, where the seal is actually sold.
  - **The profile score was real; its checklist was not.** `calculateProfileScore()` now returns `{ score, checks }` and `/business-profile-score` drives each tile from its own signal — a vendor who has completed a task sees a green tick instead of the hardcoded red "Missing" that every tile carried, and `missingCount` is derived rather than counted from literals. `DEFAULT_PROFILE_SCORE = 45` was also leaking to signed-in vendors while their data loaded; `useProfileScoreState()` now returns `isLoading` so callers render a skeleton instead of a made-up number.
  - **`MyBusiness` was an 8-item static array where every badge was a literal.** "Info Missing", "Missing", "18 Pending", "Missing" rendered identically for a fully completed vendor; "18 Pending" counted nothing at all. Each badge is now derived (unmet score signals, catalogue count, KYC state, unanswered reviews, open leads) and **a badge with no data behind it does not render**. Two route fixes: "Catalogue" pointed at `/products` (the product list, not the uploader) → `/upload-catalogue`; "KYC & Payments" pointed at `/subscription`, which does no KYC → split into **KYC → new `/kyc`** (reads `vendor_documents`) and **Payments → `/subscription`**.
  - **"Get Reviews" was actively harmful and is the single worst thing this pass removed.** It showed a hardcoded "4.2 / 24 Ratings" to every vendor, collected real customer names and phone numbers, then **discarded them** behind `toast.success("Review requests sent!")`. There is no SMS pipeline in this repo — nothing was ever sent, and the vendor had no way to know. Replaced with a shared review-link + QR block. "Reply to Reviews" rendered two **invented** reviews ("Rajesh Kumar", "Priya Sharma") and faked the submit while `/reviews` next door did the job properly; the inline component is deleted and the tile routes to `/reviews`.
  - **Both QR codes were lucide icons, and the shared link went to the wrong page.** `<QrCode>` at 128px is a *picture* of a QR code; scanning it does nothing. Added `qrcode` and both surfaces now render a real, scannable PNG with a working download. The link they shared was `${origin}/reviews` — **the vendor's own dashboard**: a buyer who followed it got their own empty seller page. It is now `${origin}/vendor/:id`, the storefront where a working `WriteReviewModal` already lives. `/reviews`' QR card also carried the literals "Fearce" and "Delhi" for every vendor, and a Download button that toasted success without producing a file.
  - **Six Korean fashion brand logos were rendering as the vendor's own categories.** `chuu-fashion`, `cherrykoko`, `brandi`, `style-nanda`, `styleonme`, `hotping` — third-party brand IP on a vendor's storefront, labelled "T-Shirts", "Denim", "Printing", under four hardcoded scroll dots that never moved. Replaced with the real `category` text[].
  - **Every other section of `/business-profile` was a demo array.** `allProducts` (six invented listings under a "Caramel Fashion" heading and a "3,538" count), `videoProducts` (five invented videos with fake durations), `recommendationProducts` (four invented products behind a drag-to-reorder grid that **persisted nothing**), two invented PDFs with non-functional Download buttons, and `ratingBreakdown` — an 80/5/0/0/15 star split shown to any vendor with **no reviews**, a fabricated reputation on their own profile. All now read real rows (`useMyProducts`, `useMyVideos` — the existing hook, reused, not a second query — `useMyCatalogues`, `useVendorReviews`), with honest empty states and a CTA. Videos show real moderation status and the moderator's rejection reason, and the modal plays the real `video_url` instead of a static thumbnail behind a decorative play button.
  - **`recommended_product_ids uuid[]` rather than `products.display_order`.** The strip is a curated **subset** in a chosen order; a global per-product ordering cannot express "these four, in this order" without implying something about the other 200, and the concern belongs to the storefront rather than to a product. Ids are resolved against the live catalogue on read, so a deleted or unpublished product drops out instead of rendering a dangling card.
  - **Fabricated identity values removed from Detailed Information.** `PAN` fell back to `"ABCPR1234D"` — a plausible-looking fake PAN presented to an unverified vendor as if it were theirs. "Cosora Member Since" was the literal `"1 Year"`; it is now derived from `created_at`. **GSTIN and CIN** are collected at signup, have columns, are already read into `VendorStoreData`, and were visible to the vendor **nowhere in the app** — while GST is shown to buyers on `/vendor/:id`. Both now render. `whatsapp` had the same problem and is now in Contact Details.
  - **Controls that did nothing are gone, not restyled.** The Month/Year selects (wired to `useState` and to nothing else), the page-level and per-product bookmarks (a `Set<number>` that reset on reload and has no meaning for a vendor on their own products), a permanent "Loading more products..." line under a fixed six-item array, the "Remove BG"/"Crop"/"Rotate" hover buttons with no handlers, a nine-square drawing of a photo gallery, the hero 3-dot menu, "Add New Business" (no handler, and `vendor_profiles.id` **is** the auth user id — one business per account, so it could never work), and the "Test Now" banner that toasted a sent notification with no push pipeline anywhere in the repo. SORT and FILTER were bare `<button>`s; they are now real controls driving the product list.
  - **Two unreachable blocks deleted, one of which the master prompt did not know about.** The second `if (showWelcome)` block (blue confetti, profile-score card) was dead behind the first — as briefed — and took `useVendorOnboardingSummary` + `vendorOnboardingSummaryFixture` ("3,302 inquiries for Fabric Wholesalers in Delhi") with it. The **inline `{currentStep === 8 && ...}` Partner Contract UI** was also dead, behind the early `if (currentStep === 8)` return, and would have started rendering the moment the steps were renumbered. `BusinessProfileEmployees.tsx` and its route are deleted after confirming zero inbound links.
  - **Six decisions were deliberately NOT made** and are recorded for a product call: contract/signature storage (**legal exposure — vendors are agreeing to a supplier agreement with no record that they did; recommend building `vendor_contracts`**), app-feedback persistence (recommend relabelling to "Contact support" unless someone will triage a queue), an offers table (recommend removing the tile), a questions/Q&A table (recommend removing the tile but roadmapping it properly — higher value than offers), `annual_turnover` (recommend adding with a range picker), and `capacity` (recommend adding `text[]` and persisting — small-batch/MOQ flexibility is a stated differentiator). In each case the **fabricated value was removed** — mandatory under the no-mock rule — without deciding the field's fate; re-adding a row is a one-line change.
  - **Also found, not fixed, flagged here:** onboarding's OTP verifies nothing (`confirmOtp` accepts any six digits, "OTP sent" sends nothing — there is no OTP backend behind this form); KYC scans land in the **public** `product-images` bucket; and step 3 still defaults `city` to `"Delhi NCR"` with a map iframe hardcoded to the same, which is the same class of fake as the ones removed but outside the listed fields.
  - **A correction to the entry below.** The previous session recorded that "`Onboarding.tsx` carries 58 type errors of its own … `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` no longer reports the 23-error baseline." That was this work mid-edit, observed from outside. It is back to **23, the unchanged baseline**; eslint is **6 errors, down from 8** (two pre-existing errors were removed with the code that caused them, and none were added).
  - **Verified through the real UI, not by SQL.** `tests/vendor-onboarding-write-path.spec.ts` drives all nine steps of the real form and then reads the database back, because a direct INSERT proves a column accepts a value — which was never the bug — and would have passed against the broken build. After one registration the count query reads **1 with_pan / 1 with_state / 1 with_pincode / 1 with_office_photos** where all four were 0, plus a non-null `vendor_documents.file_url` for the first time and a product with `unit='pieces'`, `sizes=['M','L']`, `colour='Black'` and a real image row; every stored URL is fetched and asserted `200`, so an array of `blob:` URLs cannot pass. **11/11 green** across both specs, `#ef4d62` absent from every vendor page, zero console errors. `demo-buyer` was restored byte-identical to its pre-run snapshot; `demo-vendor` was read-only. Evidence in `screenshots/vendor-*.png`; full run notes in `documentation/test.md`.
- 2026-09-08 (later): **`engagement_events` APPLIED to the live project, and a real divergence bug found and fixed in the process.** The Supabase MCP reconnected, so the migration this morning's entry had to leave written-but-unapplied is now live and verified end to end. New: `scripts/engagement-events-check.mjs`. Changed: `supabase/migrations/20260907170000_engagement_events.sql` (the status guard, added before applying), `documentation/claude.md`, `documentation/technicalimplementation.md`, `documentation/sides.md`, `documentation/test.md`.
  - **The bug: the event log did not respect the filters the counters do.** Having access meant reading the three counter RPCs for the first time instead of reasoning about them, and each is one line with a filter — `increment_product_view` only counts a `status = 'live'` product, `ad_impression`/`ad_click` only a `status = 'active'` ad. The client-side dual-write shipped this morning carried no such filter, so a **vendor previewing their own `under_review` listing would log a `product_view` against themselves while bumping no counter** — their own page refreshes quietly poisoning their own analytics, and the counter and the log permanently disagreeing about the same visit. `log_engagement_event` now repeats exactly those filters for the three event types that mirror a counter. Scoped to those three only: a `cta_click` naming a non-live product is still a real button press and is still recorded.
  - **The counter RPCs are still not rewritten, and now for a stated reason rather than an unknown one.** This morning's justification ("their bodies are not in this repo's migration history") is void — the bodies are in the migration comment now. The decision stands on its merits: folding the insert in would need a `p_source` argument on each (source is only known at the client), converting each from `language sql` to plpgsql for an exception block, and accepting that a telemetry failure rolls back the counter update it shares a transaction with. Three live functions on the buyer's critical path, rewritten for a logging side effect, is the wrong trade — the guard gets the consistency without the risk.
  - **Verified with real rows as real accounts, not by reading the schema.** `scripts/engagement-events-check.mjs` — 19/19 — writes through the real RPC as the demo buyer, vendor, admin and anon, then deletes what it wrote. It proves: direct INSERT is refused for **all** of anon, authenticated and the owning vendor (there is no INSERT policy at all); `viewer_id` comes from `auth.uid()` inside the function; `session_id` is kept when signed out and **dropped** when signed in, so a known viewer never gets a second identifier; a **forged `p_vendor_id` is ignored** in favour of the product's real owner, so traffic cannot be attributed to someone else's storefront; a bad `event_type` and an unknown product id both return cleanly rather than surfacing as a failed navigation; and on read, the vendor sees their own rows, **a buyer sees nothing — not even the events they generated themselves** — and anon sees nothing.
  - **The status guard needed a different instrument.** The demo vendor has no non-live product and a real plan trigger refuses creating one ("your free plan allows 2 listed product(s); you already have 6"), and `products` RLS hides other vendors' non-live rows from them — so the script now finds its subject through the admin client, and the ad case pauses and restores a real campaign. All five behaviours were additionally proved in a self-rolling-back `DO` block: `product_notlive_dropped=t product_live_written=t cta_on_notlive_kept=t ad_notactive_dropped=t ad_active_written=t`, with `raise` discarding every change. Confirmed afterwards: product back to `live`, ad back to `active`, zero rows left behind.
  - **End-to-end through the real app.** A buyer searching `polo` and opening a result produced `search_impression ×2` → `search_click` → `product_view`, and the `product_view` carried **`source = 'organic_search'` across the hard navigation** — the one thing the 15-second `sessionStorage` marker exists to prove. Two impressions for one search because impressions are counted **per vendor**, not per matching product, which is the intended semantics. All four probe rows deleted afterwards.
  - **The hand-written types were correct.** `src/lib/database.types.ts` was diffed token-for-token against `generate_typescript_types`: **171/171** tokens for the table and **24/24** for the function, identical including optionality markers and foreign-key names. No regeneration needed — but diff it again after any change here.
  - **Two new advisor warnings, both expected and both to be left alone.** `get_advisors` flags that `anon` and `authenticated` can execute `log_engagement_event` as a SECURITY DEFINER function. That is the design: a signed-out buyer's view must be loggable, and the function is SECURITY DEFINER precisely so no client can INSERT directly. Revoking `EXECUTE` from `anon` would silently stop recording signed-out traffic. The project already carries 67 identical warnings (`increment_product_view`, `ad_impression`, `ad_click` among them). **0 ERROR-level lints; nothing about `engagement_events` RLS is flagged.**
  - **The three-state panels flipped correctly.** Before applying, `/analytics` said "Visit-level tracking is not switched on"; after, the same panels say "No tracked activity in the last 7 days" — a genuine empty window, which is a different statement. Confirmed in a browser as the real vendor. The `installed: false` path is deliberately kept: a fresh branch database will still hit `PGRST205` and must still say "not switched on" rather than "no data".
  - **Two always-true assertions were removed from the new script before it was trusted.** A first draft printed `SKIPPED — vendor has no non-live product` and passed. A check that silently skips the exact thing it exists to verify is worse than no check, and it is the same failure this project already recorded for `bunny-e2e-check.mjs` (which once passed its delete assertion because 403 looked the same before and after). `grep -cE "check\(.*, true\)"` is now 0.
  - **Verified:** `node scripts/engagement-events-check.mjs` 19/19; `node scripts/ad-destination-check.mjs` 17/17; `npx playwright test tests/vendor-analytics.spec.ts` 5/5; `vite build` passes; zero type errors in any file this work touched.

  - **The ad→profile routing gap is fixed, and it was never really an analytics problem.** `SponsoredRail.tsx` and `NewArrivals.tsx` both did `if (a.productId) navigate('/product/${a.productId}')` for every ad regardless of placement. `Advertisements.tsx` sells a campaign goal and maps "Visit your profile" to the storePromotion and brandAd placements, and **nothing downstream read that** — so a vendor paying ₹99/day to send buyers to their storefront received product traffic, and a profile-goal ad with no `product_id` was a card that navigated nowhere at all. `adDestination()` now branches on placement. This changes real buyer-facing navigation, so it is recorded as a business rule in `claude.md`, not just here.
  - **`placement` is a comma-joined CSV and the test suite now proves the branch knows that.** Live rows read `"openListing,trustedSeal"` — `razorpay-verify-payment`'s `adRows()` does `spec.placementIds.join(",")` — so the check is membership, never equality. The decision lives in `src/lib/adDestination.ts`, a deliberately **import-free** module, so `node scripts/ad-destination-check.mjs` can transpile that one file with esbuild and exercise it with no browser, session or database. That indirection earns its keep immediately: `active_ads` returns **zero rows** today (both demo campaigns ended in July), so no UI test could reach this branch at all. 17/17 pass, including `notStorePromotionReally` → false, which guards against a substring test creeping back in.
  - **One table, seven event types — not four tables, and the reasoning is in the migration.** Every panel this feeds is "group this vendor's events by `<dimension>` over `<window>`". Four tables would mean four near-identical schemas, four RLS policies to keep in step, four `(vendor_id, created_at)` indexes and a UNION in every query that spans them — and the queries do span them: "ad-attributed profile views" is an ad event and a profile event at once. The type-specific columns (`product_id`, `ad_id`, `query_text`, `cta_name`) are all nullable and cheap.
  - **There is deliberately no INSERT policy at all.** Writes go only through `log_engagement_event`, a SECURITY DEFINER function mirroring `increment_product_view`. `viewer_id` is read from `auth.uid()` **inside** the function and is not a parameter — the anon key ships in the bundle, so a client that could name the viewer could forge every unique-visitor and attribution figure on the page. The vendor is derived server-side from the product or ad wherever one is named, so traffic cannot be attributed to someone else's storefront. SELECT is `(vendor_id = auth.uid()) or is_admin()`: a buyer cannot read the events they generated, which is the point — `viewer_id` must not become a way to enumerate who looked at what.
  - **The counters are not touched, and specifically not by rewriting the three RPCs.** The master prompt's wording ("in the same function") was implemented at the client call site, not in SQL: the bodies of `increment_product_view`, `ad_impression` and `ad_click` are **not reproduced anywhere in this repo's migration history**, and `create or replace`-ing a live function from a guess at its body is how a `status = 'live'` filter silently disappears from production. Each call site now performs both writes side by side, and ProductDetail shares the **same** `cosora.viewed.<id>` session dedup key for both — two different dedup rules would make the counter and the event log disagree about the same visit.
  - **`source` is resolved with a 15-second marker, and the TTL is the load-bearing part.** The value is known at the origin (the search page knows the click was a search result) but needed at the destination (ProductDetail logs the view). Threading it through would mean adding router state to every `<Link to="/product/...">` in the buyer app and keeping them all in step. `markNavSource()` / `consumeNavSource()` use `sessionStorage` so the marker survives a hard navigation — but without the TTL a stale marker from a search ten minutes ago would relabel a later direct visit as organic search, which is worse than the honest `'direct'` default.
  - **Search impressions are counted per VENDOR, not per product.** The panel answers "did buyers searching `<term>` see me", and a vendor with six matching products did not get six impressions from one search. Deduped per (query, vendor) for the life of the mount — re-filtering or re-sorting the *same* result set is not a new search. Only a typed `?q=` fires it; a `?category=` arrival is a different intent, not a search term. The panel is labelled as the vendor's **own** impressions only and makes no platform-wide rank claim, because the sample cannot support one.
  - **Ad-attributed profile views and product views are two numbers that are never summed.** They are different outcomes bought by different campaign goals, and one merged figure would hide exactly the thing a vendor who paid for "Visit your profile" needs to check. Unique visitors are likewise shown **alongside** total views rather than replacing them, labelled as a lower bound — signed-out viewers can only be deduped within a session.

Collapsing those two is how a broken page passes for an empty one — the project's existing rule, applied to a new surface. Traffic Sources is back as a real card on this basis; Performance Trends and daily views are rebuilt from real timestamps and are driven by the range filter.
  - **CTA tracking is additive everywhere it was added** — `message`, `view_reviews` (ProductDetail), `ask_more_reviews`, `upload_more_photos` (CompetitorAds), `promote_product` (Analytics), `add_product` (the seller quick-actions grid, opted in by name so a new tile is never silently tracked). Every button does exactly what it did and also reports that it was pressed. The Button Performance panel folds the existing `calls` data in as one more row, and says that its history goes back further than the others', rather than reporting Call Now on a separate surface.

Changed: `src/pages/Analytics.tsx` (rewritten), `src/pages/Advertisements.tsx`, `src/pages/Quotes.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/products.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDashboard.ts`, `src/lib/queries/rfqs.ts`.
  - **What was actually fake, confirmed by reading the file before touching it.** `Analytics.tsx` held five hardcoded chart constants — `viewsData` (Jan 1200 → Jul 3800), `weeklyData`, `sourceData` (a fixed 40/30/20/10 traffic split), `categoryData` ("Cotton Fabrics 35%" — not a category in this taxonomy), `inquiryData` — plus a `topProducts` fixture, a shadowed top-level `stats` fixture, and a Ratings Snapshot card reading `4.2 / 5`, `24 reviews • 312 helpful votes`, `Premium Cotton Blend`, `Response rate 93%`, `+8% this month`. `Advertisements.tsx`'s six-tile `STATS` constant was invented end to end. The KPI cards, Top Performing Products and the AI Insight line were already real and were left alone.
  - **The master prompt's premise for item 1.4 was wrong, and the correction is the more useful change.** It said to reuse "Quotes.tsx's existing Total Order Value computation". There was no computation: `Quotes.tsx` printed a hardcoded `₹24.5L` in two places, alongside `Acceptance Rate 25%`, `Avg. Response Time 1.5 days` and `This Month: 8 quotes`. So the computation was built once in `useVendorOrderValue` and **both** pages now read it — Analytics gets its card and Quotes' four fixtures become four counted figures. An accepted quote is the order (there is no orders table): value is `quotes.price_per_unit × rfqs.quantity`, and an accepted quote whose RFQ carried no quantity contributes **zero** and is surfaced as `unpriced` rather than guessed at.
  - **"Missed Calls" is not a metric this schema can produce, so it prints an em dash and says why.** `calls` is exactly `(id, buyer_id, vendor_id, direction, product_context, created_at)` — verified against the live project, not inferred. A row records the **dialer opening**, not a connected call; a web app can only emit a `tel:` URL. There is no status, duration or answered flag to count. Every row in the live table is also `direction = 'outgoing'`, because the insert policy is `buyer_id = auth.uid()` and `useCallBuyer` deliberately does not log — so `callAnalytics.ts` maps direction to the **vendor's** perspective (a buyer's outgoing call is the vendor's inbound one) instead of printing the raw value, and still reads correctly if that policy is ever widened.
  - **"Avg. Cost/Lead" is deleted, not renamed, and must not come back.** Cosora ads are flat-rate prepaid placements — no bid, no auction, no attributed order revenue — so cost-per-lead, CPC, CPM and ROAS are all figures the schema cannot support. The replacement is **revenue booked ÷ leads**, matching Cosora-Admin's `AdsMonitoring.tsx` wording, vendor-wide on the Advertise strip and **per campaign** on Analytics' Ads tab.
  - **Attributing ad money per campaign needed a join that does not exist.** `ad_orders` has no `ad_id`; one order publishes one `advertisements` row per item in its spec (`razorpay-verify-payment`'s `adRows()`), all sharing the order's placement CSV and window. So an order's amount is divided evenly across its spec items and each item matched to the campaign promoting that product whose `starts_at` is nearest the payment, claiming each campaign at most once; whatever cannot be matched is reported as `unattributed` so the per-campaign column always reconciles with the vendor-wide total. **`ad_orders.amount` is in paise** (`computeAmountPaise` multiplies by 100) — every read divides. Campaigns published through the demo path (`publishDemoAds`, no Razorpay secret) create **no `ad_orders` row at all**; those genuinely booked ₹0 and show ₹0. Do not back-fill them from the price table to make the column look populated.
  - **The time filter used to be decorative; now every card declares which kind of metric it is.** Two kinds exist and the difference is structural, not cosmetic: *windowed* metrics sit on a real per-row timestamp (`quotes.created_at`, `messages.created_at`, `reviews.created_at`, `calls.created_at`) and genuinely re-scope; *lifetime* metrics sit on monotonic counters with no event history (`products.views_count`, `products.enquiries_count`, `advertisements.impressions/clicks`) and cannot. `increment_product_view`'s signature is `{ p: uuid }` — no timestamp, no viewer, nothing to window. Each card therefore carries either a `Last <range>` pill or a `Lifetime` pill with the reason on hover. A filter click never silently does nothing.
  - **Three charts are stated as unavailable rather than silently deleted.** Performance Trends, Weekly Views and Inquiries & Conversions all needed a per-day series that no counter here can produce, so they render a panel explaining that a day-by-day trend needs one row per view with its own timestamp. A chart vanishing from the top of a page reads as a bug; a chart saying why it is empty does not. Traffic Sources is removed outright (item 1.3) with a comment in place pointing at its real replacement.
  - **A 15,425% "conversion rate" was rendering, and that is now caught.** `views_count` and `enquiries_count` are independent counters not necessarily incremented over the same period, so their ratio can exceed 100% — this vendor's does, by two orders of magnitude. Above 100% the card shows the two counts it is built from and says the counters are not comparable. A four-figure conversion rate is not a conversion rate, and printing one would undermine every honest figure beside it.
  - **Response time is a threshold percentage, not an average, on purpose.** Per conversation, the buyer's first message and the vendor's first reply after it give one real first-reply delay from `(conversation_id, sender_id, created_at)` alone — `messages` has no response-time column and no `read_at`. The headline is "X% of buyer messages get a first reply within 24 hours" (the Etsy/Amazon framing) with mean and median secondary, because one week-old unanswered thread would drag an average into meaninglessness while twenty other replies went out in minutes. A thread opened **more than** 24h ago with no reply counts as a miss, not as missing data — dropping it would quietly reward ignoring buyers. A thread opened inside the last 24h is still in flight and is excluded from the percentage but still shows in the "waiting on a reply" action list. One query feeds both the headline panel and the action list.
  - **`profileScoreSignals()` makes the profile-score nudge structurally unable to disagree with the dashboard ring.** `calculateProfileScore` was thirteen inline `if` tests; it now sums the signal list that the nudge also reads, so the two surfaces evaluate the same conditions once. Same weights, same verdicts, one place. (`href: "/add-product"` was corrected to `/products` — there is no `/add-product` route.)

A three-tier "For You" feed — taste vector → onboarding-preference centroid → global popularity — replaces the keyword-substring category match.** Three new migrations (`20260907160000_pref_category_map.sql`, `20260907160100_buyer_taste_and_cold_start.sql`, `20260907160200_for_you_products.sql`, all **applied and recorded**); `src/lib/queries/forYou.ts` (new), `src/pages/ForYou.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/products.ts`, `src/lib/buyerCategories.ts`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`.
  - **Which tier actually runs today: popularity, for all five buyers.** `products.embedding` is 0/30 — OpenAI billing is still inactive (`20260907120300`) — so tier 1 and tier 2 are both unreachable. Measured, not assumed: `buyer_taste_embedding` and `buyer_cold_start_embedding` return NULL for every buyer, and `for_you_products` returns 20 rows of popularity for each. The never-empty guarantee is the only part of this feature currently observable.
  - **The brief's core SQL could not compile, and this is the substantive finding.** pgvector 0.8.2 defines only `halfvec * halfvec` (ELEMENT-WISE), `+` and `-`. There is **no `halfvec * float8` and no `halfvec / float8`** — checked against `pg_operator` before writing anything — so `sum(embedding * w) / nullif(sum(w), 0)` has no valid parse. `sum(halfvec)` and `avg(halfvec)` do exist, so only the scaling needed solving: `halfvec_scale(v, k)` multiplies element-wise by a constant-filled vector of the same dimension. Exact, not an approximation. It is `STRICT` so a null weight short-circuits instead of `array_fill` building an array of NULLs that the `::vector` cast rejects — which is what preserves the intended divide-by-zero guard.
  - **`resolvePreferredCategoryIds` does not exist and never has.** The brief names it in `src/lib/queries/products.ts`; it is not there or anywhere else. A stale comment in `buyerCategories.ts` referenced it, which is presumably where the expectation came from — that comment is now corrected. The only consumer of `PREF_TO_DB_CATEGORY_NAMES` is `preferredVideoCategoryNames()`, which targets `product_videos.category` text, not `products.category_id`. So the "pass resolved ids in from the frontend" option had no implementation behind it, and the lookup table was the only real choice.
  - **That TS map was also stale, which would have made cold-start return NULL for every buyer who has preferences.** Its targets are legacy flat names; the 2026-09-07 taxonomy migrations moved all live inventory onto the tree, so `T-shirts/Tops`, `Shirt`, `Dress`, `Ethnic Wear`, `Trousers`, `Jeans` and `Kidswear` now hold **zero live products**. `Activewear` and `Footwear` are worse — **ambiguous**, matching both a dead legacy row and a live tree child. `pref_category_map` therefore keys on `categories.id`, not on a name, because a name can no longer identify a category.
  - **The map is a translation of the existing vocabulary, not a redefinition** — every row is the tree successor of a legacy name the TS map already pointed at. 21 rows covering 23 of 26 live products: `tshirts` → Men's/Unisex T-Shirts + Women's Tops (5); `dresses` → Women's Dresses + Women's Ethnic Wear (7); `bottomwear` → Men's Jeans + Men's/Women's Pants-Trousers (3); `activewear` → Activewear (3); `shirts` → Men's Shirts (2); `accessories` → Footwear + all 10 Fashion Accessories children (2); `kidswear` → Kids Wear (1). `coords` and `fabrics` stay deliberately unmapped — no such category exists, exactly as the TS map's empty arrays already said. Winter Wear (3 live) maps to no preference, same as before.
  - **All three tiers verified against synthetic one-hot-per-category vectors, then fully rolled back.** Taste fired for the buyer with 8 views + a targeted RFQ (top hits Winter Wear @0.819, Footwear @0.843 — both categories he actually viewed). Popularity fired for the buyer with neither history nor preferences. And deleting the one view of the buyer whose prefs are `[tshirts]` dropped her from `taste` to **`cold_start` → Men's T-Shirts**, which is the direct proof the map repair was necessary: on the old legacy names that call returns NULL.
  - **Correction to the brief's expectation: cold-start will fire for ZERO of the five buyers, not "almost every buyer".** The two buyers with preferences set both also have view history, so taste wins for them; the two with no history have no preferences either, so popularity wins. Once embeddings exist the split is **3 taste / 0 cold-start / 2 popularity**. Cold-start only becomes reachable for a buyer who completes onboarding and has not yet viewed anything. (Also: `recently_viewed` is **14** rows, not 13.)

  - **`for_you_products` returns `source`** so the client can tell a real recommendation from a leaderboard. Labelling a popularity feed "picked for you" would be a lie, and re-deriving the tier client-side would be a second source of truth. `distance` is NULL on the popularity tier rather than 0, which would read as a perfect match.
